SETUP (about 15 min) =======================\\n# Read the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-use-hardware and aii-json first.\\n# WS = this workspace; R = the run root. Every write goes under WS.\\nuv venv; uv pip install numpy pandas pyarrow scipy scikit-learn statsmodels python-igraph leidenalg interpret loguru matplotlib pyahocorasick nltk joblib\\n# Copy (do not import across workspaces) into WS/lib/ and record the sha256 of each copy in WS/results/provenance.json:\\n#   EXP5 matcher.py, rangefile.py, common.py (rename to common5.py), lexicon_v1.parquet (check the sha against frozen_lexicon.sha256),\\n#        results/source_field.parquet, snapshot/works_manifest.json\\n#   EXP3 features.py (rename to ego.py), common.py (rename to common3.py), backbone/slice*.npz, scan/topic_ids.json,\\n#        results/topic_meta.csv\\n#   EXP6 lib/h2.py, lib/stats_core.py, inputs/field_backbone.json\\n# If R is not mounted: re-download the manifests with EXP5's restore logic (snapshot/works_manifest.json lists the 2,040 S3\\n#   keys; public, zero credit), rebuild the frame from the definitions, and log everything in WS/results/deviations.json.\\nframe = read_csv(EXP5/frame_concepts.csv)                       # 12,499 rows\\nassert counts by split/group == {DEV 4771, PHYS 742, LIFEENV 1113, SOC 1352, MATHDEC 165, COHORT 4356}\\nframe['cohort_part'] = where(split == COHORT, 'COH_DEVHOME' if group in {CS, Eng, BGM, Med} else 'COH_OTHER', NA)\\nframe['in_exp6'] = concept_id in EXP6/results/frame_concepts.csv   # report the overlap count\\n\\n# ======================= 1. PASS A: grounded frame matches with work/topic/author IDs =======================\\n# One zero-credit pass over all 2,040 files, adapted from EXP5 scan_full.process_file. It runs in the BACKGROUND\\n# (PID-managed, resumable per file) while steps 3-4 are coded.\\nCOLS_A = EXP5.COLS + ['id', 'topics.list.element.id', 'authorships.list.element.author.id']\\n# EXP5.COLS already has title, year, type, is_paratext, is_xpac, source.id, topics field id, primary_topic field id,\\n# concepts id/score. There is no referenced_works here: Pass B handles citations.\\nper file fi:\\n   tb = read_columns(key, size, COLS_A, n_threads=8)\\n   base, vfield, tagstate: computed EXACTLY as in EXP5 (same automaton built from the FULL lexicon_v1, same TAG_MIN = 0.3)\\n   (a) BG[year, topic] += base works per topic per year, and GT[year] += base works with >= 1 topic\\n       (a full-corpus background for 1995-2022; it extends EXP3's ckpt)\\n   (b) h = verified hits with tagstate == 1 AND ci in the frame set.\\n       COUNTS[ci, year, vfield] += 1 for all years  -> used by the reproduction check\\n   (c) for hits with t0[ci] - 3 <= year <= t0[ci] + 2: emit a row\\n       (ci, year, work_id int64, vfield, topic_idx list[int16] via topic_ids.json, author_ids list[int64] only if year >= t0)\\n   (d) RSAMPLE: base works with year 2003..2016 and splitmix64(fi, row) % 400 == 0 (about 0.25%, about 250k works):\\n       emit (work_id, year, vfield). This is the citation reference set for normalising O4.\\n   write the parts to WS/passA/parts/*.parquet and done_{fi}.json\\nmerge -> WS/data/frame_matches_early.parquet (split into parts < 90 MB), bg_topics.npz, counts_check.parquet, ref_sample.parquet\\n# Expected size: about 12.5k concepts x about 150 early works = about 2M rows; topics about 3 ids, authors about 5 ids\\n# -> roughly 100-200 MB of parquet.\\nCHECK A1 (STOP rule): for every frame concept, Spearman over years of COUNTS[ci, year, :].sum() against grounded counts from\\n   EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1). The median must be >= 0.99 AND recomputed early_volume must equal frame.early_volume for\\n   >= 99% of concepts. Otherwise STOP, log the mismatch and diagnose (automaton, lexicon hash, base filter) before any feature.\\nCHECK A2: for the years both cover, BG must reproduce EXP3 ckpt bg with Spearman >= 0.999. Unknown topic ids are counted and logged.\\n\\n# ======================= 2. PASS B: citations received by early works (O4) =======================\\ntargets = sorted int64 array of (early work ids, i.e. year in t0..t0+2 of frame concepts) UNION ref_sample ids\\nCOLS_B = ['publication_year', 'type', 'is_paratext', 'is_xpac', 'referenced_works']\\nper file: citing = base works (same filter) with year 2003..2022; flatten referenced_works -> int64 via utf8_slice at 'W';\\n   m = isin(refs, targets) (np.searchsorted on the sorted targets)\\n   CIT[target_id, citing_year] += 1 (sparse; emit (target_id, citing_year, n))\\nmerge -> WS/data/cites_early.parquet   # EXP6 read referenced_works for all files in 12.7 min\\n# If Pass B has not finished by T+3h15m: drop O4, log it, continue.\\n\\n# ======================= 3. FEATURE MATRIX (window t0..t0+2 ONLY) =======================\\n# Per-concept yearly per-field grounded counts n_cj(t) come from EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1, vfield > 0 = labelled).\\n# home = frame.home (a field code; for intersection-born, parse all home codes the frame records).\\nFAMILY E  (reference / popularity; 'E' rows): log_count, share, growth_ind, accel, burst, logvol, growth_c\\n   (from concept_features_basic)\\n   + author_growth = log(1 + distinct authors t0+2) - log(1 + distinct authors t0)                         (Pass A)\\n   + n_authors_early = log(1 + distinct authors t0..t0+2)\\n   B5 = [logvol, growth_c, offhome_share, entropy, reach]  # identical to iterations 1-2; B5 is the baseline, not a candidate\\nFAMILY F  (disciplinary): lab_entropy, lab_reach, lab_offhome_share, log_offhome_volume (reuse)\\n   + rao_stirling = sum_{i != j} p_i p_j (1 - phi_n[i, j]), with phi_n = phi rescaled to [0, 1]; labelled works t0..t0+2\\n   + fields_gained_per_yr = (|ENTERED(t0+2)| - |ENTERED(t0)|) / 2\\nFAMILY G  (landing): G, G_A, G_btw, G_deg, G_phimin, REL_home, RS (reuse; flag 'previously_scored_heldout')\\nFAMILY FR (retained frontier, D3 verbatim via h2.states() on n_cj(t) restricted to t0..t0+2):\\n   CONTACT_REACH = #off-home fields with >= 1 labelled paper in t0..t0+2\\n   RETAINED_REACH = #off-home fields with >= 2 papers in at least 2 of the 3 years\\n   RETENTION_RATIO_early = RETAINED_REACH / max(CONTACT_REACH, 1)  (NA if CONTACT_REACH = 0 -> 0 plus a missing flag)\\n   FRONTIER_POTENTIAL = sum over k not in ENTERED(t0+2), k != home, of mean_{j in early-retained} phi[j, k]\\n                        (0 if there are no early-retained fields)\\n   also D_rca_end, D_vol_end, M0_density_end at t0+2 (the D3 formulas), kept for the portability table only\\nFAMILY A  (co-occurrence ego network, art_yrradSC27HtQ features.concept_core adapted):\\n   works = [(year, topic_idx tuple)] from frame_matches_early for t0-3..t0+2\\n   windows: PRE = t0-3..t0-1, W1 = {t0}, W2 = {t0+1}, W3 = {t0+2}   # DEVIATION: the EXP3 W3 = t0+3..t0+4 would leak past the window\\n   new_edge_rate = (M / 3) / (n1 + 1)                                # the window is 3 years, not 5\\n   background: bg_window() from Pass A's BG/GT (not EXP3's ckpt, whose year range may stop early)\\n   SELF topics: the EXP3 rule, with the concept name plus aliases_used from the frame\\n   slice_of(): the EXP3 SLICES (2000-04, 05-09, 10-14); years 2015-16 clamp to slice 2 (log it)\\n   outputs kept (about 26): D_z, D_ratio, D_rare, D_sub, D_obs(=M new neighbours), NOV, NOV_res, F_res, F_z,\\n     deg_W1, deg_W3, deg_growth, str_growth, new_edge_rate, edge_persistence, turnover, participation, n_comm_W3,\\n     comm_entropy (Shannon entropy of W3 neighbour-community weights; new, 3 lines), comm_transitions,\\n     ego_density_W3, ego_density_change, btw_t4 (rename btw_end), btw_change, kcore_end, constraint_end, constraint_change\\n   dropped as near-duplicate variants: D_lag, D_q, D_withself, F_bg (logged)\\n   SPEED: N_NULL = 200 (EXP3 used 200 for its reliability splits); betweenness with cutoff = 4 on the kNN backbone.\\n   Timing test on 60 random concepts first (see testing_plan); extrapolate over 7 workers x 4 vCPU.\\n   If the projection exceeds 100 min: keep ALL held-out-group concepts (3,372) and a stratified random 2,000 DEV plus\\n   2,000 COHORT (seed 20260928, stratified by group x t0), and log it.\\n   Parallelism: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 4 workers), with the context loaded once per worker as in EXP3.\\nFAMILY S  (social; candidate S, Cheng-style): for off-home labelled early works (vfield not home, t0..t0+2):\\n   build a graph with authors as nodes and a clique per paper; S_comp = #connected components / #off-home papers;\\n   S_comp_n = #components / #distinct off-home authors; also S_isolated_share.\\n   If author ids are missing for > 50% of those works, compute S only where coverage >= 50% and log coverage.\\n   If the id column is unreadable, drop family S and log it.\\nOUTPUT: WS/results/indicator_matrix.parquet (ci x about 52 indicators + family tags) and indicator_dictionary.csv\\n   (name, family, window, formula, source file, expected sign from F3 where it exists, pre-registered prediction flag).\\nDIAGNOSTICS on DEV only: missing rates; Spearman of each indicator with logvol and growth_c (the size diagnostic,\\n   flag |rho| > 0.6); the indicator x indicator Spearman matrix with average-linkage clustering on 1 - |rho|\\n   -> results/indicator_corr_dev.csv and figures/indicator_clusters.png.\\n\\n# ======================= 4. OUTCOMES (one table, one fold assignment) =======================\\nO1c  = log(1 + N_grounded t0+6..t0+8) - log(1 + N_grounded t0..t0+2)   (continuous; the direction's definition; from agg_counts)\\nO1b  = concept_outcomes.O1 (EXP5 binary sustained-share rule; secondary, AUC)\\nO2r_m50 (primary breadth), O2r_m30 = concept_outcomes (exact hypergeometric, no truncation)\\nO2r_resid = O2r_m50 - (a + b * logvol), with a and b fitted by OLS on DEV ONLY, frozen and reported next to EXP5's constants\\nO3   = concept_outcomes.O3 (the art_33 transience rule verbatim; report base rates per group)\\nO4   = log((1 + C_late / 6) / (1 + C_early / 3)) summed over the concept's early works, where C_early = citations in\\n       t0..t0+2 and C_late = citations in t0+3..t0+8 (cites_early),\\n       MINUS the same quantity for the ref_sample works matched on (publication year, venue field), taking the\\n       expectation over the concept's early-work mix of year x field. This is field-and-year normalised\\n       (an MNCS-style expected value).\\nO5   (art_O7Dq4L02QnDN): join on concept_id == int(openalex_id.strip('C')); fall back to qid_resolved; report the join rate.\\n     Events used: year_usable == True AND relation == 'same', from the sources MeSH (DateIntroduced), Wikipedia creation\\n     (exact or calibrated estimate), Wikidata P571/P575, taxonomy_added_between (ACM CCS, MSC, PACS/PhySH), and curated\\n     lists EXCEPT Clarivate/CAS Research Fronts, which are citation-derived and circular with O4.\\n     AT RISK: no qualifying event with year < t0 and no mesh_baseline flag. Concepts not at risk are set to NA for O5.\\n     O5 = 1 if any qualifying event has t0 <= year <= t0+8 (MeSH and taxonomy require year > t0).\\n     O5_WW = the same, using Wikipedia and Wikidata only; defined for every group.\\n     Report the base rate and the at-risk n per group. A group with < 20 positives is DROPPED from O5 scoring (O5_WW too).\\n     Because the Wikipedia creation wave is 2001-07, the O5/O5_WW baselines also include onset-year dummies (B5+t0).\\n     Sensitivity: relation in {same, narrower}.\\nOUTCOME SEAL: write WS/data/outcomes_dev.parquet (DEV rows) and WS/data/outcomes_sealed.parquet (HELDOUT + COHORT rows).\\n   Log the sha256 of the sealed file. lib/seal.py load_heldout() raises unless results/frozen_spec.json exists and its\\n   hash matches logs/seal.log, and it refuses a second call (the EXP5 seal.py pattern).\\n   NOTE in the README: EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts for its H1/H3 tests. Held-out is fresh for\\n   every indicator EXCEPT the G family.\\n\\n# ======================= 5. DEV SELECTION (DEV rows only) =======================\\nContinuous outcomes {O1c, O2r_m50, O2r_resid, O4}:\\n   psp(x, y | B5) = Pearson( resid(rank x ~ rank B5 + group dummies + t0 dummies), resid(rank y ~ same) )\\n   CI: 1,000 concept-bootstrap resamples; the residualisation is REFITTED in each resample (the concept is the unit).\\nBinary outcomes {O1b, O3, O5, O5_WW}:\\n   dAUC = AUC(B5 + x) - AUC(B5), for L2 logistic models evaluated leave-one-DEV-group-out (4 folds by home group) with\\n   out-of-fold predictions; x is standardised on the training folds. CI: 1,000 concept-bootstrap resamples refitting both\\n   models (a vectorised Newton-IRLS from EXP5 models.py).\\nPer outcome, frozen ranking rule:\\n   eligible = indicators with DEV CI excluding 0 and missing <= 30%\\n   order by |estimate| descending; greedy de-duplication: skip x if |Spearman(x, already selected)| > 0.85 on DEV\\n   top10[outcome] = the first 10; if fewer than 10 are eligible, fill by |estimate| (flag 'filled')\\n   store the frozen SIGN of each selected indicator\\nunion_top10 = the 10 indicators with the best mean rank across the 8 outcomes (same de-duplication)\\nSensitivity rankings (reported, not used to freeze): given B5 + label_coverage_early + tag_coverage + precision_c;\\n   and ranking by AUC of top vs bottom tercile within group.\\nLEARNED MODELS (all DEV, hyperparameters by leave-one-DEV-group-out CV):\\n   continuous: ElasticNetCV(l1_ratio in {0.1, 0.5, 0.9, 1.0}) on standardised indicators + B5;\\n     ExplainableBoostingRegressor(interactions=10, outer_bags=8, random_state=20260928)\\n   binary: LogisticRegressionCV(penalty='l1', solver='saga', Cs=20);\\n     ExplainableBoostingClassifier(interactions=10, outer_bags=8)\\n   imputation: DEV medians plus missing flags for indicators with > 5% missing; the scaler is frozen\\n   saved: learned_model.json (coefficients, the L1 path over C, EBM term importances, shape-function tables for the\\n     top 8 terms, pairwise terms), models/*.joblib\\nPOWER (DEV simulation): plant partial rho = 0.05 / 0.10 in the held-out group sizes; report P(CI > 0) per group and pooled.\\n   Expected SE of partial rho: about 0.037 (PHYS), 0.03 (LIFEENV), 0.027 (SOC), 0.078 (MATHDEC), 0.017 (pooled).\\n   Store these as MDE = 2.8 x SE in frozen_spec.\\nFREEZE: results/frozen_spec.json = {indicator list and formulas, window definitions, N_NULL, B5, covariate sets,\\n   the O2r_resid a/b, the O5 rules, top10 per outcome with signs, union_top10, learner hyperparameters, scaler constants,\\n   imputation medians, bootstrap B and seed, Holm families, DL method, pre-registered predictions P1-P5 (below),\\n   sha256 of every lib/*.py and of indicator_matrix.parquet}\\n   -> write sha256(frozen_spec.json) to logs/seal.log; git-commit the code; ONLY THEN call load_heldout() (once).\\n\\n# ======================= 6. HELD-OUT SCORING (once) =======================\\nunits = [PHYS, LIFEENV, SOC, MATHDEC, COH_DEVHOME, COH_OTHER]\\nfor each outcome, for each indicator in top10[outcome] (and union_top10), for each unit:\\n   continuous: psp given B5 + t0 dummies within the unit; concept bootstrap, B = 1,000\\n   binary: dAUC using the FROZEN DEV coefficients (B5 model and B5 + x model) applied to the unit. CI: joint refit\\n     bootstrap (resample DEV concepts -> refit -> resample unit concepts -> score), B = 1,000\\n   DL-pool the 4 held-out groups (Fisher z for rho; dAUC with bootstrap SE) -> pooled estimate, 95% CI, tau2, I2\\n   sign agreement with the frozen sign: count over 4 groups + 2 cohort parts; exact sign test p\\nHolm within each outcome family (the 10 pooled tests of that outcome's top 10), using p from the pooled z.\\nLEARNED vs SINGLE vs B5 on the SAME held-out units:\\n   continuous: Spearman(pred, y) and out-of-sample R2 of B5-only ElasticNet vs ElasticNet-all vs EBM vs B5 + best single\\n     (the best single is the DEV #1 indicator, frozen); paired concept bootstrap for the differences\\n   binary: AUC, delta-AUC, Brier score and calibration slope for the same four models\\nPORTABILITY TABLE (all about 52 indicators, x 4 DEV groups + 4 held-out groups + 2 cohort parts): psp with O2r_m50, O2r_resid\\n   and O1c, plus the raw Spearman with O2r_m50. Rows outside the top 10 are labelled EXPLORATORY.\\n   -> portability_table.csv (indicator, family, unit, outcome, n, rho, ci_lo, ci_hi, frozen/exploratory,\\n      previously_scored flag)\\nPRE-REGISTERED PREDICTIONS (frozen before the unseal; evaluated on the pooled held-out groups):\\n   P1 entropy, D_rare, D_ratio, participation and NOV_res: raw Spearman with O2r_m50 > 0 (CI > 0) in >= 3 of 4 groups;\\n      AND for D_rare, D_ratio, participation and NOV_res: pooled psp | B5 CI upper bound < 0.10 ('add little').\\n      Entropy is in B5, so only its raw part is testable.\\n   P2 edge_persistence: pooled raw rho and psp with O2r_m50 both < 0.\\n   P3 deg_growth, str_growth, new_edge_rate: FAIL held-out = pooled psp CI includes 0 OR a sign flip in >= 2 of 4 groups.\\n      Also report their CS-only DEV values.\\n   P4 RETENTION_RATIO_early and FRONTIER_POTENTIAL: pooled psp > 0 with CI > 0 for O2r_resid AND O1c.\\n   P5 CONTACT_REACH: pooled psp CI includes 0. Because B5 contains reach, ALSO report P4/P5 given B5-minus-reach, so the\\n      test is not trivial by construction.\\n   Each prediction gets a verdict (HOLDS / FAILS / UNTESTABLE) in rq1_heldout.json.\\nSENSITIVITIES (post-seal, labelled): excluding in_exp6 concepts; excluding intersection-born; label-coverage covariates\\n   added; O5 relation in {same, narrower}; O2r_m30.\\n\\n# ======================= 7. EXPLAIN, FIGURES, OUTPUTS =======================\\nfigures/portability_heatmap.png (+pdf): indicator (rows, grouped by family) x unit; colour = psp with O2r_m50; a dot where\\n   the CI excludes 0; frozen top-10 rows in bold\\nfigures/heldout_forest_<outcome>.png: per-unit and DL-pooled estimates with CIs for the top 10\\nfigures/learned_vs_single.png: bars of held-out delta-AUC / delta-Spearman with CIs for the models, the best single and B5\\nfigures/ebm_shapes.png: the top 6 EBM shape functions for O2r_resid and O5_WW\\nfigures/indicator_clusters.png (DEV correlation dendrogram)\\nfigures/o5_base_rates.png\\nCase exemplars: for the best portable indicator, list the 3 held-out concepts with the highest and the lowest predicted\\n   O2r_resid, with their top-10 W3 ego neighbours (features.py _top_nb_W3) -> results/case_exemplars.json\\nWrite: indicator_matrix.parquet, outcomes.parquet (merged after the unseal), rq1_dev_selection.json, frozen_spec.json\\n   (+ logs/seal.log), rq1_heldout.json, portability_table.csv, learned_model.json, deviations.json, provenance.json,\\n   method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out: one example per concept with input = the indicator vector, output = outcomes,\\n   predict_B5 / predict_best_single / predict_EBM for every outcome; DEV rows are out-of-fold)\\n   + full/mini/preview variants (aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if > limit).\\nREADME.md (layout, how to run, results table, 'Restoring removed files') and .aii/manifest.yaml:\\n   keep: data/frame_matches_early*.parquet, cites_early.parquet, bg_topics.npz, results/, figures/, models/;\\n   delete: .venv/ (regenerable: uv venv + uv pip install -r requirements.txt); passA/parts/ and passB/parts/\\n   (regenerable: uv run passA.py / passB.py); __pycache__/.\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"F1 SNAPSHOT UNREACHABLE OR TOO SLOW for Pass A (projected > 90 min after 40 files):\\n(a) First reduce COLS_A: drop author ids, which drops family S (log it).\\n(b) If still too slow, process a deterministic 50% of files (every even file index). This is a random subsample of works, so ego counts are halved. Mark it in deviations and keep the EXP5 agg_counts reproduction check on the sampled files only (expected ratio about 0.5).\\n(c) If the S3 snapshot is entirely unreachable: family A cannot be computed. Deliver the RQ1 matrix from families E, F, G and FR and B5 (about 26 indicators, all computable from agg_counts and concept_features_basic). Add a coarse FIELD-level co-occurrence ego proxy from agg_counts ptfield (the primary-topic field of each grounded work) against the 26-field co_by_year background: new-field-neighbour count, persistence, participation over the phi-Leiden communities of the 26-field backbone. State plainly that the fine topic-level ego network was not recomputed.\\n\\nF2 CHECK A1 FAILS (median Spearman < 0.99 against agg_counts). Diagnose in this order: lexicon sha, automaton built from the full lexicon, base-type filter, TAG_MIN, venue lookup. Fix and rerun on 5 files before continuing. Never proceed with an unreproduced frame.\\n\\nF3 PASS B DOES NOT FIT (not finished by T+3h15m, or targets > 5M ids): drop O4 and log it. As a secondary substitute, use the snapshot's present-day cited_by_count of early works, normalised by the ref-sample year x field mean. That column is cheap and could be added to Pass A as 'cited_by_count'; mark it as NOT windowed. It has no role in selection. If both are missing, O4 is dropped from all tables.\\n\\nF4 EGO FEATURES TOO SLOW (timing test > 100 min projected): apply in order\\n(i) N_NULL 200 -> 100;\\n(ii) betweenness cutoff 4 -> 3, or replace it with the degree of the inserted node in the kNN graph;\\n(iii) the stratified subsample (all held-out concepts, 2,000 DEV, 2,000 COHORT).\\nEach step is logged; held-out groups are never subsampled.\\n\\nF5 O5 SPARSE: if fewer than 2 held-out groups have >= 20 positives for O5, the taxonomy/MeSH O5 is reported descriptively only, and O5_WW becomes the only confirmatory recognition outcome. If O5_WW is also < 20 in a group, that group is dropped from O5 scoring and the base rates are reported. If the join rate is < 90%, try the qid_resolved join, then report the unmatched concepts.\\n\\nF6 DEGENERATE BINARY OUTCOMES: O3 or O1b with < 20 positives in a group means that group is dropped for that outcome (O3 was all-Medicine in iteration 1; check the base rates first).\\n\\nF7 EBM / interpret INSTALL FAILS: use sklearn HistGradientBoosting with monotone-free settings plus permutation importance and 1-D partial dependence as the interpretable substitute, and log it.\\n\\nF8 TIME PRESSURE at T+4h45m: skip the sensitivities and case exemplars. The sealed held-out scoring of the top 10, the learned-vs-single comparison, the portability table, the prediction verdicts, the README and the manifest are mandatory and come first.\\n\\nF9 AN INDICATOR IS ALL-NaN IN A HELD-OUT GROUP (e.g. ego features need M >= 3): report n and mark it UNTESTABLE for that group. Never impute outcomes, and never impute features on held-out with anything but the frozen DEV medians.\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"T0 UNIT TESTS (no network, < 5 min; results/unit_tests.json).\\n(1) h2.states() on a hand-made 5-field x 6-year count matrix: ENTERED/RETAINED/LOST are correct, including the '>= 2 years earlier' retained rule and the off-home restriction.\\n(2) RETAINED_REACH and FRONTIER_POTENTIAL on a toy phi (3 fields) against hand values.\\n(3) Partial Spearman equals the Pearson of rank residuals; on data where y = f(B5) + noise with x independent, the mean psp over 200 simulations is within 0.01 of 0.\\n(4) The dAUC pipeline with a planted effect (x adds 0.5 SD log-odds) returns dAUC > 0.02; with shuffled x, |mean| < 0.005.\\n(5) The DL pooling routine reproduces a textbook example (EXP6 stats_core, or metafor's dat.bcg log RR: tau2 = 0.313).\\n(6) The seal gate raises if frozen_spec is missing and raises on a second unseal.\\n(7) The O5 rule on 4 synthetic concepts: an event before t0 makes the concept not at risk; t0+8 is inclusive; Research Fronts are ignored.\\n(8) Ego features on EXP3's P78 dev concepts with the ORIGINAL EXP3 windows reproduce EXP3 results/features*.csv (D_ratio, participation, NOV_res; Spearman >= 0.95 after title-match -> TAG differences). This validates the port before the window change.\\n\\nT1 PASS A MINI (3 files: take indices 65, 1125 and 1407, which EXP5 used for its stage tests).\\n- Runtime per file gives an extrapolation over 2,040 files at 4 workers. Proceed if <= 70 min, else fallback F1(a).\\n- The per-file hit counts for frame concepts with tagstate == 1 must EXACTLY equal the counts implied by EXP5 scan/stage_test_parts/agg_{fi}.npz (decode uC keys: ci, year, vfield, ptfield, tagstate, mt).\\n- Topic id mapping: < 1% unknown ids.\\n\\nT2 PASS A FULL: check A1 (median Spearman >= 0.99; early_volume equal for >= 99%) and check A2 (background Spearman >= 0.999 against EXP3 ckpt). Report the numbers in results/checks.json.\\n\\nT3 PASS B MINI (3 files): some early works receive citations; citing years are >= the cited year for > 99.5% of links; the per-file runtime extrapolates to <= 25 min.\\n\\nT4 EGO TIMING AND SANITY on 60 random DEV concepts:\\n- time per concept, projected to the full frame;\\n- no all-NaN column;\\n- M (new neighbours) has median > 3. If the median is <= 3 with 1-year windows, do NOT use overlapping windows. Instead lower the neighbour threshold n_ck >= 2 to >= 1 for W windows only, and log it (decided on DEV before freezing);\\n- spot-check the _top_nb_W3 of 5 known concepts (e.g. optogenetics, microRNA, if they are in the frame) for sensible neighbours.\\n\\nT5 DEV-ONLY SANITY BEFORE THE FREEZE:\\n- B5 alone reproduces the EXP5/EXP3 magnitude (DEV Spearman of the B5 ridge prediction with O2r_m50 about 0.7-0.83; report it).\\n- The size diagnostic table.\\n- The indicator correlation clusters show >= 6 distinct clusters at |rho| < 0.7 (families are really distinct; if not, say so).\\n- Shuffled-outcome placebo on DEV: 20 permutations of O2r_resid within group. The number of indicators with DEV CI excluding 0 must be about 5% of about 52 (<= 6 on average); otherwise the bootstrap is anticonservative, so fix it before freezing.\\n- Learned-model LOGO-CV on DEV beats B5 by a stated margin, or this is reported as a null.\\n\\nT6 PRE-UNSEAL CHECKLIST (logs/seal.log):\\n- no held-out/cohort outcome column is present in any object built before the freeze (grep the pickles/parquets written before the freeze for the sealed column names on held-out ci);\\n- git commit hash;\\n- frozen_spec sha.\\n\\nT7 POST-SCORING AUDIT (independent code path, audit.py):\\n- recompute the pooled psp for the top 3 indicators of O2r_resid with scipy/pingouin-free code (own ranks, own OLS);\\n- recompute held-out AUCs with sklearn roc_auc_score;\\n- they must match to 1e-6;\\n- shuffled-outcome control on held-out: 20 within-group permutations give pooled |psp| < 0.03 on average;\\n- a planted positive control is detected.\\n\\nT8 OUTPUT VALIDATION: aii-json validates method_out.json against exp_gen_sol_out; every file is < 100 MB (aii-file-size-limit); README numbers are cross-checked against rq1_heldout.json by a small script that asserts equality.\",\n  \"domain_practice\": \"WHAT I READ: the run's own prior artifacts on disk (EXP5 README, frozen_spec.json and scan_full.py; EXP3 features.py and config; EXP6 README and deviations; Eval1 F3 portability; the O5 dataset README); art_dxvRpQufMR0e's positioning summary (Guevara 2016 entry AUCs 0.68-0.90; the Weng 2013 precedent; the ANS template); and two targeted searches: Small, Boyack & Klavans 2014 (Research Policy 43:1450-1467), and the forecasting-evaluation literature (the Porter et al. 'Measuring tech emergence: A contest', TFSC 2020; the 2023 'What's next? Forecasting scientific research trends', Heliyon; the 2026 AI phase-transition early-warning preprint arXiv 2606.12828, which freezes thresholds on 2017-21 and scores 2023-25 with precision/recall against a 13.5% base rate). No domain handbook fits scientometrics, so the practice below rests on these sources and the run's measurements.\\n\\nBASELINES. Every emergence-indicator paper reports popularity/growth references: counts, growth, Kleinberg bursts (Kleinberg 2003), and the Porter/Carley emergence score, whose ingredients are novelty, persistence, growth and community. The first comparison a reviewer names is 'does it beat simple growth and volume?'. The 'Measuring tech emergence' contest and Maillart 2026 both show that growth alone explains much of the outcome. In diversification work the reviewer's first rival is relatedness density (Hidalgo 2007; Guevara 2016). Fair tuning means the baseline gets the same window, the same learner and the same CV as the candidate. Here that baseline is B5 (volume, growth, off-home share, entropy, reach) plus log_count/burst/accel/author growth as separate rows, fitted with the same L2 logistic or rank residualisation.\\n\\nDATA. Standard corpora are WoS/Scopus clusters (Small et al. 2014 use co-citation threads of about 14 papers per year), MAG/OpenAlex concepts (the arXiv 2606.03864 link forecasting), and topic-level OpenAlex. Legacy OpenAlex concepts (MAG FoS) are deprecated but still in the snapshot, and they are seeded from Wikipedia, which is a known selection condition. Paper-level topic classifiers used as discipline labels are circular for diffusion, which is why venue labels are used for features.\\n\\nCONTROLS. Hold the feature window fixed and separate it from the outcome window (a gap of 3 or more years). Hold the onset cohort fixed or control for it, because field growth and database coverage change by year. Hold field size fixed through rarefaction or residualisation. The confound most likely to catch this design is VOLUME: a structural indicator that relabels early size. The second is label coverage: Eval1 found all G-variant O1 gains were coverage artefacts.\\n\\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Concept-level forecasting papers use hundreds to thousands of topics. Results with fewer than about 30 units per domain are dismissed (iteration 1's 47-concept panel was underpowered: rho_B5 = 0.83 left no room). Readers expect a CI, not a p-value alone, and per-domain results next to pooled ones. The emerging expectation is a temporal hold-out with frozen thresholds (arXiv 2606.12828). Effects already on record here are small (EXP5 H3 held-out partial rho about 0.03-0.07; EXP3 out-of-group psp for D_ratio 0.34 on 47 concepts, which will shrink). The held-out n here (742/1,113/1,352/165 plus a 4,356-concept cohort) gives an SE of about 0.017 pooled, so a partial rho of about 0.05 is detectable pooled. MATHDEC (SE about 0.08) can only confirm large effects. That is stated up front, not discovered.\\n\\nMEASURES AND REPORTING. For binary outcomes: AUC and delta-AUC over the baseline, or precision@k against the base rate. For continuous outcomes: Spearman or partial correlation. Report both global and per-field results, a random-effects pooled estimate with I2 (standard in this run and in meta-scientometrics), multiplicity correction, and a table of all indicators, not only the winners. Validation against EXTERNAL recognition (MeSH, Wikipedia, curated lists) is the gold standard that Rotolo et al. 2015 and Small et al. 2014 call for. It is rarely done because base rates are low and year-dependent.\",\n  \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(1) Growth/volume baselines are in every comparison: B5 is the conditioning set for every indicator, and popularity rows (count, burst, acceleration, author growth) compete as candidates. The learned models are compared against B5 with the SAME learner.\\n(2) Feature and outcome windows are separated: features use t0..t0+2 only; outcomes use t0+6..t0+8 (O4: t0+3..t0+8). The EXP3 W3 window (t0+3..t0+4) is explicitly cut to avoid leakage.\\n(3) Volume confounding is handled by rarefied O2r, residualised O2r_resid, a DEV size diagnostic per indicator, and partial correlations given B5.\\n(4) Label coverage is a pre-declared sensitivity covariate set (the lesson of Eval1 D).\\n(5) Domain hold-out plus temporal hold-out: 4 held-out home groups, plus a 2010-14 cohort split into DEV-home and non-DEV-home parts. This separates 'new time' from 'new domain'.\\n(6) Per-group results, DL pooling with I2, sign counts, Holm within outcome families, concept-clustered refit bootstrap CIs, and the full portability table for all about 52 indicators. Domain-specific failures are reported, not averaged away, and one is pre-registered (P3).\\n(7) External recognition (O5) is joined for the first time, with at-risk sets and onset-year controls for the Wikipedia wave.\\n(8) The freeze and hash seal before scoring follow the frozen-threshold practice of recent early-warning work.\\n\\nDEPARTURES AND THEIR COST:\\n(a) The held-out outcomes are not virgin. EXP5 already unsealed O1/O3/O2r for these concepts to test H1 (field retention) and H3 (G family). The ~45 other indicators were never scored on them, and no selection here touches held-out rows, so the practical leakage is limited to the G family, whose held-out rows are flagged 'previously scored'. Cost: a reader may call the held-out 'second-use'. It is disclosed in the README and in rq1_heldout.json.\\n(b) Onset t0 = the first year with >= 20 TAG-grounded title matches (the EXP5 frame), not a Porter/Carley emergence score or co-citation threads. Concepts are legacy OpenAlex concepts, which carry a Wikipedia-seeding selection. This is justified because it is the run's single frozen frame, and changing it would break the 'one outcome table, one fold assignment' requirement. Cost: the results hold for named concepts, not for unnamed research fronts.\\n(c) The ego windows are one year each (W1 = t0, W2 = t0+1, W3 = t0+2) instead of EXP3's 2+1+2 years, so they fit the 3-year feature window. Cost: noisier early ego statistics, especially for small concepts. It is mitigated by the T4 check and a DEV-decided threshold fallback, all before the freeze.\\n(d) N_NULL = 200 and a betweenness cutoff, for compute reasons. Cost: small extra noise in D_z and a local, not global, betweenness. Both are logged as deviations.\\n(e) The primary association measure is partial Spearman, not precision@k or AUC, for the continuous outcomes. This matches the direction and the run's iteration-2 practice. precision@top-decile is added to rq1_heldout.json for O2r_m50 and O5_WW so the result can be compared with the forecasting literature. It is cheap and closed inside the plan.\\n(f) No Porter/Carley emergence-score baseline is computed. Its ingredients (novelty = newborn onset, persistence = O1-like, growth, community = author growth) are covered by family E rows, and a composite would add little. Cost: a reviewer may ask for it by name. This is noted as a limitation.\\n(g) Venue labels come from each work's first source, and conference-heavy CS is under-covered (EXP5 deviation: base types article|review only). Cost: DEV CS indicators are measured on a thinner slice, which could make the pre-registered 'CS-only' results partly a coverage artefact. The label-coverage sensitivity addresses this.\\n(h) O5 uses year_usable Wikipedia estimated dates and taxonomy intervals. The dates are coarse, and the Wikipedia creation wave of 2001-07 correlates with t0, so onset-year dummies enter the baseline. Cost: O5 is weaker as ground truth for early-onset concepts. This is reported with base rates by t0.\\n(i) MATHDEC (165 concepts) is underpowered for effects below about 0.2, so its group-level result is reported as uninformative when its CI is wide, not as a failure.\"\n}\n", "numLines": 12, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 12}}

+ 4]}\n\n\ndef lgC(n: float, k: float) -> float:\n    from scipy.special import gammaln\n    return gammaln(n + 1) - gammaln(k + 1) - gammaln(n - k + 1)\n\n\ndef set_context(ctx: dict) -> None:\n    \"\"\"ctx: nt, years (list), bg [len(years), nt], Gt {year: n}, comm/comm_q/deg/knn/full_edges per slice, subfield,\n    names, ldf (topic lemma df), tlem (topic lemma sets), lemmas min(chunk, n - s)\n        g = lw[None, :] + rng.gumbel(size=(m, len(pool_idx)))\n        top = np.argpartition(-g, M - 1, axis=1)[:, :M]\n        L = np.sort(lab[top], axis=1)\n        out[s:s + m] = 1 + (np.diff(L, axis=1) != 0).sum(axis=1)\n    return out\n\n\ndef f_null(p_mix, pool, T1, T3, nc1, nc3, nbg1, N1, nbg3, N3, rng, n):\n    if len(pool) == 0 or T1 == 0 or T3 == 0 or nc1 == 0 or nc3 == 0:\n        return np.full(n, np.nan)\n    pr = p_mix[pool] / p_mix[pool].sum()\n\n    def S(T, nc, nbg, N):\n        X = rng.multinomial(T, pr, size=n).astype(float)\n        with np.errstate(divide=\"ignore\", invalid=\"ignore\"):\n            P = np.log(X * N / (nc * nbg[pool][None, :]))\n        elig = (X >= 2) & np.isfinite(P) & (P > 0)\n        key = np.where(elig, X + with np.errstate(invalid=\"ignore\"):\n            return np.nanmean(np.where(np.isfinite(Ps), Ps, np.nan), axis=1)\n    with warnings.catch_warnings():\n        warnings.simplefilter(\"ignore\",   return 0.0, 0, float(\"nan\")\n    g = knn_graph(s).copy()\n    g.add_vertices(1)\n    v = g.vcount() - 1\n    g.add_edges([(v, int(k)) for k in idx])\n    n = g.vcount()\n    b = sp1 = np.nansum(P[\"W1\"][NB[\"W1\"]])\n    sp3 = np.nansum(P[\"W3\"][NB[\"W3\"]])\n    r[\"str_growth\"] = math.log(sp3 + 1) - math.log(sp1 + 1)\n    n_years = len(early_years)\n    r[\"new_edge_rate\"] = (M / float(n_years)) / (n1 + 1)\n\n    def jac(a, b):\n        u = (a | b).sum()\n        return (a & b).sum() / u if u else float(\"nan\")\n    with ins = np.zeros(C[\"nt\"], dtype=bool)\n            ins[idx] = True\n            e = int((ins[a] & ins[b]).sum())\n            r[f\"ego_density_{w}\"] = e / (len(idx) * (len(idx) - 1) / 2)\n grounded (tagstate 1) frame hits keyed (ci, year, vfield) for years 2000-2016 -> check A1\n  EARLY rows       grounded frame hits with t0-3 <= year <= t0+2: (ci, year, work_id, vfield, topic idx Path\n\nsys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent / \"lib\"))\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nimport pyarrow as pa\nimport pyarrow.compute as pc\n\nfrom common import (DATA, INPUTS, MATCH_Y0, MATCH_Y1, NY, PASSA, TAG_MIN, Y0, Y1, add_deviation, load_frame, mix64,\n                    setup_logger, source_field_lut, works_files, write_parquet_parts)\n\nCOLS = [\"title\",    \"concepts.list.element.score\",\n        \"id\", \"topics.list.element.id\", \"authorships.list.element.author.id\", \"cited_by_count\"]\nRS_MOD = 400\n_W: dict = {}\n\n\ndef _init() -> None:\n    from matcher import build_automaton\n    lex = pd.read_parquet(INPUTS / \"lexicon_v1.parquet\", columns=[\"concept_id\", \"forms\", \"mtypes\"])\n    entries = [(f, ci, m) for ci, (fs, ms) in enumerate(zip(lex.forms, lex.mtypes)) for f, m in zip(fs, ms)]\n    A, specs = build_automaton(entries)\n    sid, code = source_field_lut()\n    fr = load_frame()\n    t0_of = np.full(len(lex), -1, np.int64)\n    t0_of[fr.ci.to_numpy()] = fr.t0.to_numpy()\n    tids = np.asarray(json.loads((INPUTS / \"topic_ids.json\").read_text()), np.int64)\n    order = np.argsort(tids)\n    _W.update(A=A, specs=specs, cid=lex.concept_id.to_numpy(np.int64), sid=sid, code=code, t0_of=t0_of,\n              tids_sorted=tids[order], tids_pos=order.astype(np.int64), nt=len(tids))\n    pa.set_cpu_count(1)\n\n\ndef _oa_int(arr, prefix_len: int = 22, null: str = \"https://openalex.org/X0\") -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"'https://openalex.org/W123' -> 123 (int64); null -> 0.\"\"\"\n    s = pc.utf8_slice_codeunits(pc.fill_null(arr, null), prefix_len)\n    return pc.cast(s, pa.int64()).to_numpy(zero_copy_only=False)\n\n\ndef _field_code(arr) -> np.ndarray:\n    s = pc.utf8_slice_codeunits(pc.fill_null(arr, \"https://openalex.org/fields/10\"), 28)\n    v = pc.cast(s, pa.int64()).to_numpy(zero_copy_only=False) - 10\n    return np.clip(v, 0, 26).astype(np.int64)\n\n\ndef _list_offsets(col) -> tuple[pa.Array, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"(flattened values, offsets[n+1]) of a list column; nulls count as empty lists.\"\"\"\n    arr = col.combine_chunks() if isinstance(col, pa.ChunkedArray) else col\n    ln = pc.fill_null(pc.list_value_length(arr), 0).to_numpy(zero_copy_only=False).astype(np.int64)\n    off = np.zeros(len(ln) + 1, np.int64)\n    off[1:] = np.cumsum(ln)\n    return pc.list_flatten(arr), off\n\n\ndef process_file(fi: int, key: str, size: int) -> dict:\n    from common5 import surf_arrow\n    from matcher import Returns boolean [NY, 26] matrices (years Y0..).\"\"\"\n    x = g[:, 1:]\n    cum = np.cumsum(x, 0)\n    entered = cum >= min_n\n    w3 = x.copy()\n    w3[1:] += x[:-1]; w3[2:] += x[:-2]\n    ent_lag2 = np.zeros_like(entered); ent_lag2[2:] = entered[:-2]\n    offhome = np.ones(26, bool)\n    for h in home:\n        offhome[h - 11] = False\n    retaining = ent_lag2 & (w3 >= min_n) & offhome[None, :]\n    lost = entered & (w3 == 0)\n    return {\"entered\": entered, \"retaining\": retaining, \"lost\": lost, \"w3\": w3, \"cum\": cum, \"offhome\": offhome}\n\n\ndef rca_entered(g: np.ndarray, GF: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"entry when cumulative count >= 2 and the field's cumulative share of the concept exceeds its share of all works.\"\"\"\n    x = np.cumsum(g[:, 1:], 0)\n    tot = x.sum(1, keepdims=True)\n    F = np.cumsum(GF, 0)\n    share_all = F / np.maximum(F.sum(1, keepdims=True), 1)\n    share_c = x / np.maximum(tot, 1)\n    ok = (x >= 2) & (share_c > share_all)\n    return np.maximum.accumulate(ok.astype(int), 0).astype(bool)\n\n\ndef build_risk_sets(frame: pd.DataFrame, G: dict[int, np.ndarray], bb: dict, GF: np.ndarray,\n                    entry_def: str = \"count\", horizon: int = 8) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Rows = (concept, year t, candidate field k not entered by t-1, not home). {
  "title": "Does closing up at home slow a concept's spread?",
  "summary": "MECHANISM test of the openness lead (RQ2 timing and 'why it works'), within concepts, on the EXP5 frame (12,499 TAG-grounded concepts; DEV 4,771; old held-out PHYS 742 / LIFEENV 1,113 / SOC 1,352 / MATHDEC 165; COHORT 4,356). One zero-credit pass over the OpenAlex S3 snapshot (adapted EXP8 passA.py: same matcher, TAG rule and venue lookup; title matching widened to 2000-2022; grounded frame hits kept for t0-3..min(t0+10, 2022) with work id, venue field, topic ids, author ids and document type). From these the executor builds a concept x year panel (t0..t0+10, about 120k rows). Each row holds HOME-ONLY 1-year ego-network statistics computed with a yearly port of EXP8 lib/ego.py on the EXP3 Leiden backbone: new-partner rate, n_comm, participation, ego density (raw and size-adjusted against a degree-matched null), edge persistence and the OPEN_home composite. Controls: home and total volume, log degree, fields still at risk. The outcomes are the next-year count of NEW off-home fields ENTERED (D3 states) and a binary any-entry indicator. The specification is hash-sealed BEFORE the outcome columns are attached. Estimands: (a) PPML with concept and year FE, entries(t+1) on home-only density(t) (prediction beta < 0) and on OPEN_home(t) (beta > 0), with concept-clustered CIs and 2,000 concept-bootstrap refits. The reverse path, density(t+1) on entries(t), must be weaker in standardised terms (paired bootstrap). A Sun-Abraham interaction-weighted event study around the first home-only CLOSURE JUMP, with leads -3..-1 and lags 0..+4, a pre-trend joint test, and event-date and field-label permutation placebos. (b) A partner-source decomposition of the early new_edge_rate / n_comm_W3 signal: method vs domain topics (LLM-labelled OpenAlex topics, benchmarked, cap $1), home vs off-home partner field, old vs new Leiden community, home vs off-home carrying venue. Also a bridging-paper profile. (c) The request's sequence question: does a home-prominence peak precede off-home take-off, and do intersection-born concepts take off without one? DEV, old held-out and COHORT are reported separately and labelled mechanism evidence, not confirmation. Planned cost: 0 OpenAlex credits, <= $1 OpenRouter, about 5.5 h on cpu_plus.",
  "runpod_compute_profile": "cpu_plus",
  "builds_on": "This plan DEEPENS the run's existing line. It is iteration 4's within-concept mechanism artifact for the one lead that survived held-out testing (EXP8, art_dFQ6jbgNsR6Q): six openness components of the early co-occurrence ego network predict held-out size-adjusted breadth given B5 (new_edge_rate +0.118; n_comm_W3 +0.167; participation +0.150; NOV_res +0.139; ego_density_W3 -0.102; edge_persistence -0.080), and RETENTION_RATIO_early is negative (-0.120). The sibling Art 1 tests replication and confounds on a fresh cohort. This artifact asks the within-concept timing question those cross-sectional correlations cannot answer, where concept type and pre-onset footprint are absorbed by concept fixed effects by construction.\n\nEverything is read by absolute path from the run root R = /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/ (all files verified present when this plan was written). The only declared dependency is art_O7Dq4L02QnDN (concept key, used for Wikidata descriptions in the topic-typing prompt and for the external-recognition columns of case exemplars).\n\n(1) FRAME, SPLIT, COUNTS: R/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_5/ (art_wxWssKSUR45f).\n- frame_concepts.csv: ci, concept_id, qid, name, level, aliases_used, t0, newborn, home ('|'-separated field codes 11..36), n_home, weak_home, intersect40, intersect25, home_top_share, group, split in {DEV, HELDOUT, COHORT}, precision_c, label_coverage_early, tag_coverage, early_volume, in_P78. This is the authoritative 12,499-row frame. Intersection-born = intersect40 == 1 (>= 2 homes).\n- scan/agg_counts.parquet (ci x year 1995-2022 x venue field x primary-topic field x tagstate x match type): the reproduction target for the new pass, and the source of D3 counts.\n- scan/year_field_totals.npz; results/source_field.parquet (source -> venue field); snapshot/works_manifest.json (2,040 S3 keys).\n\n(2) PASS AND EGO CODE: R/3_invention_loop/iter_3/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_8/ (art_dFQ6jbgNsR6Q).\n- passA.py (Pass A template: COLS, _oa_int, _field_code, _list_offsets, process_file, resumable parts; it ran in 62 min on cpu_plus). lib/matcher.py, lib/rangefile.py, lib/common.py, lib/common5.py, lib/common3.py.\n- lib/ego.py: concept_core(), rq1_windows(), neighbours(), pmi(), self_topics(), bg_window(), window_counts(), _centrality(), EGO_OUT. lib/ego_ctx.py builds the context (backbone slices, bg, lemmas). It was validated against EXP3 to 1e-15 (results/t0_8_ego_port.json).\n- data/frame_matches_early/part_*.parquet (t0-3..t0+2 grounded hits with work, topic, author ids): used as the equality check for the new pass on the overlapping years.\n- data/features_basic.parquet (B5), data/ego_features.parquet (static ALL-PAPERS OPEN components), data/outcomes.parquet (O2r_m50, O2r_resid, O1c for all splits; already unsealed), data/bg_topics.npz (BG[year, topic], GT[year], 1995-2022).\n- results/o2r_resid_fit.json, results/case_exemplars.json, results/frozen_spec.json (static OPEN signs, the reference for the yearly port).\n\n(3) D3 STATES: R/3_invention_loop/iter_3/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_7/ (art_22ppE1snfHKj).\n- results/state_panel_{dev,heldout}.parquet: ci, concept_id, field, year (t0-3..2022), n, cum, w3, rca_1y, state (0 untouched, 1 entered, 2 retained, 3 lost, 4 home), age_since_entry. Authoritative D3 states.\n- lib/d3.py (panel_states, rca_panel) and lib/h2_exp6.py (states(): entered = cum >= 2; retaining = entered two years earlier AND w3 >= 2 AND off-home; lost = entered AND w3 == 0). These are used to fill any frame concept missing from the state panels, and to validate equality where both exist.\n\n(4) TOPIC BACKBONE: R/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/ (art_yrradSC27HtQ): backbone/slice0-2.npz (topic PMI slices 2000-04 / 05-09 / 10-14; Leiden gamma 3 communities comm; kNN edges ka/kb; full edges a/b; deg), scan/topic_ids.json, results/topic_meta.csv (topic -> subfield, field, name).\n\n(5) FIELD BACKBONE: R/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_6/inputs/field_backbone.json (26-field PMI phi, 1998-2002), lib/h2.py, lib/stats_core.py (DerSimonian-Laird pooling, bootstrap helpers).\n\nNEGATIVE FINDINGS BUILT PAST (not re-tested): the gateway retention lead (EXP5 H1 dAUC -0.00001); A*_h; the retained-frontier account (EXP7: volume-matched null, reversal under min-cp); the relay and rescue mechanisms (EXP6: not supported). The Exp6 two-class typology (HMM ARI 0.094) is not assumed. Art 3 handles typology.\n\nDIVISION OF LABOUR WITH SIBLINGS: Art 1 freezes the static OPEN z constants on the EXP5 frame, in parallel. This artifact cannot wait for them, so it freezes its OWN yearly z constants (DEV concept-years, features only, before outcomes are attached) and logs that as a deviation from the strategy text. yearly_panel.parquet is written so that iteration 5 and Art 3 can reuse it.",
  "implementation_pseudocode": "# ======================= 0. SETUP (about 15 min) =======================\n# Read the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-use-hardware, aii-json, aii-file-size-limit\n# and aii-openrouter-llms first. WS = this workspace; R = run root. Every write goes under WS. The OpenAlex API is NOT used\n# (0 credits); only the public S3 snapshot via HTTP range reads.\nuv venv; uv pip install numpy pandas pyarrow scipy scikit-learn statsmodels pyfixest python-igraph leidenalg loguru\n   matplotlib pyahocorasick nltk joblib\n# Copy (do not import across workspaces) into WS/lib/, recording sha256 per copy in WS/results/provenance.json:\n#   EXP8: passA.py -> passM.py, lib/{matcher,rangefile,common,common5,common3,ego,ego_ctx,stats_core,seal}.py\n#   EXP7: lib/d3.py, lib/h2_exp6.py\n#   EXP3: backbone/slice*.npz, scan/topic_ids.json, results/topic_meta.csv\n#   EXP5: lexicon_v1.parquet (check the sha against frozen_lexicon.sha256), results/source_field.parquet,\n#         snapshot/works_manifest.json\nframe = read_csv(EXP5/frame_concepts.csv)   # assert 12,499 rows; split x group counts == {DEV 4771, PHYS 742,\n                                             # LIFEENV 1113, SOC 1352, MATHDEC 165, COHORT 4356}\nframe['home_list'] = [int(h) for h in home.split('|')]; frame['multi_home'] = intersect40 == 1\nframe['body'] = DEV | OLD_HELDOUT (split == HELDOUT) | COHORT\nframe['h_end'] = min(t0 + 10, 2022)        # COHORT concepts have shorter panels (t0 2010-14 -> 8-12 years); logged\n\n# ======================= 1. PASS M: one zero-credit snapshot pass (about 70-90 min, in the BACKGROUND) =======================\n# Adapt EXP8 passA.process_file. The base filter, automaton, stemmed verification, TAG_MIN = 0.3 and venue lookup are\n# UNCHANGED. Changes:\n#   (i) title matching for publication years 2000..2022 (was 2000..2016)\n#   (ii) keep rows for frame hits with tagstate == 1 and t0 - 3 <= year <= min(t0 + 10, 2022)\n#   (iii) add the column 'type' (article | review) to the emitted row\n#   (iv) authors for ALL kept years (was year >= t0 only); cited_by_count is dropped (not needed)\n#   (v) BG/GT are NOT recomputed; reuse EXP8 data/bg_topics.npz (1995-2022, same filter) -> faster\nCOLS_M = passA.COLS minus 'cited_by_count' plus 'type'\nrow = (ci int32, year int16, work_id int64, vfield int8, doc_type int8 {0 article, 1 review}, topic_idx list[int16],\n       author_ids list[int64])\nwrite WS/passM/parts/part_{fi}.parquet + done_{fi}.json (resumable); run with ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers),\n   8 range-read threads per worker; PID file + nohup; poll the log every 5 min\nmerge -> WS/data/frame_matches_long/part_*.parquet (each < 90 MB). Expected: 12.5k concepts x about 600 works = about\n   7-8M rows, about 0.6-1.0 GB of parquet -> kept as parts; aii-file-size-limit applies.\nCHECK M1 (STOP rule): per concept, grounded counts per (year, vfield) from Pass M must EQUAL EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1)\n   for every year <= 2022, for >= 99.5% of concept-years; the median per-concept Spearman over years must be >= 0.999.\n   Otherwise STOP, diagnose (lexicon sha, automaton, year window, base filter) and rerun on 5 files before continuing.\nCHECK M2: for t0-3..t0+2 the (ci, work_id) set equals EXP8 frame_matches_early for >= 99.5% of rows, and the topic lists\n   are identical.\n# While Pass M runs: write steps 2-4 and the unit tests; run the topic typing (step 5a), which needs no pass.\n\n# ======================= 2. D3 STATE PANEL (about 10 min) =======================\nsp = concat(EXP7 state_panel_dev.parquet, state_panel_heldout.parquet)\nmissing = frame concepts absent from sp (EXP7's DEV panel may be 'EXP5 minus EXP6'; log the count)\nfor missing concepts: G = agg_counts (tagstate == 1) -> [NY, 27] per concept; states via d3.panel_states / h2_exp6.states\n   (min_n = 2), the SAME code; VALIDATE by recomputing 200 random concepts already in sp: every cell must be equal.\nPer concept-year t, over off-home fields:\n   entries(t)   = #fields whose state switches to 'entered' (cum reaches 2) in year t\n   any_entry(t) = entries(t) > 0\n   at_risk(t)   = #off-home fields still untouched at the end of t-1  (the risk-set size; 26 - |home| - already entered)\n   cum_entries(t-1), retained(t), lost(t)\n\n# ======================= 3. YEARLY HOME-ONLY EGO PANEL (features only; about 40 min on 6 workers) =======================\n# A new function ego_year(ci, t) in WS/lib/ego_yearly.py, built from ego.py primitives (neighbours, pmi, bg_window,\n# window_counts, self_topics). NO betweenness. Context via ego_ctx with EXP8 bg_topics.npz.\nworks_home(ci)  = [(year, topic_idx)] for grounded works with vfield in home_list (vfield == 0 unlabelled -> excluded;\n                  log home-paper coverage = home works / all grounded works per concept-year)\nworks_all(ci)   = all grounded works (for the ALL-PAPERS comparison build)\nSELF(ci) = ego.self_topics(name, aliases, counts over t0..t0+2 of works_all)   # frozen once per concept, as in EXP8\ns(t) = slice_of(t) using EXP3 SLICES; years >= 2015 clamp to slice 2 (2010-14) -> logged; sensitivity drops those rows\nfor t in t0..h_end:\n   NB(t)   = neighbours(cnt_home[t], nc_home[t], bg[t], GT[t], SELF, min_n = 2)    # 1-year window, as EXP8 W1..W3\n   SEEN(t) = union of topics with >= 1 home count in t0-3..t-1\n   NEW(t)  = NB(t) & ~SEEN(t)\n   new_rate(t)   = |NEW(t)| / (|NB(t-1)| + 1)                                      # yearly analogue of new_edge_rate\n   n_comm(t)     = #distinct comm[s(t)] labels among NB(t)\n   participation(t) = 1 - sum_c w_c^2, with w_c = count-weighted share of NB(t) in community c\n   nov_res(t)    = share of NEW(t) outside the concept's first-year (t0) modal community, minus the degree-weighted\n                   expectation over the pool (EXP8 NOV_res formula, applied to NEW(t))\n   density(t)    = (#full_edges[s(t)] among NB(t)) / C(|NB(t)|, 2)      (NA if |NB(t)| < 2)\n   dens_null(t)  = mean density of 100 random topic sets of size |NB(t)| drawn with bg[t]-proportional weights from the\n                   non-SELF pool (Gumbel top-k as in ego.distinct_null); dens_adj(t) = density(t) - dens_null(t)\n   persistence(t)= Jaccard(NB(t-1), NB(t))\n   deg(t) = |NB(t)|, kcore(t) = coreness of the concept node inserted into the kNN graph of s(t) (igraph coreness only)\n   controls: log1p home works(t), log1p all works(t), log1p deg(t), log(at_risk(t)), concept age a = t - t0\n   ALSO for works_all: density_all(t), new_rate_all(t) (the ALL-PAPERS build, for comparison only)\nOPEN_home(t) = mean of z(new_rate), z(n_comm), z(participation), z(nov_res), -z(density), -z(persistence); needs >= 4 of 6.\n   The z constants are the mean and SD over DEV concept-years, computed from FEATURE columns only and frozen in step 4.\n   (DEVIATION: the strategy asked for Art 1's EXP5 static constants. Art 1 runs in parallel and the yearly components\n   have a different scale, so own constants are frozen here and logged.)\nParallelise over concepts: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers), context loaded once per worker; chunks of 200 concepts;\n   checkpoint per chunk. Timing test on 100 concepts first (T4) -> extrapolate.\nWRITE WS/data/yearly_features.parquet (no outcome columns).\nPORT CHECK: aggregating ego_year over t0..t0+2 with EXP8 definitions (windows W1..W3 = t0, t0+1, t0+2, all papers)\n   must reproduce EXP8 ego_features new_edge_rate, n_comm_W3, participation, ego_density_W3, edge_persistence to 1e-9 on\n   300 random concepts. Otherwise STOP and fix the port.\n\n# ======================= 4. PRE-REGISTRATION AND SEAL (before any outcome join) =======================\nWS/prereg.md + WS/results/frozen_spec.json:\n   panel definition, the yearly formulas, the yearly z constants, the closure-jump rule, the take-off rule, the prominence\n   definition, the estimators, controls, FE, clustering, bootstrap B and seed (20260929), Holm families, the predictions\n   and verdict rules below, the sha256 of every WS/lib/*.py file and of yearly_features.parquet.\n   sha256(frozen_spec.json) -> WS/logs/seal.log; git commit. ONLY THEN does the estimation script attach D3 outcomes\n   (lib/seal.py gate: attach_outcomes() raises unless seal.log matches frozen_spec).\n   HONEST NOTE in prereg.md: EXP7/EXP8 already looked at D3 states and static breadth for these concepts. The within-\n   concept yearly estimand was never estimated, and this seal controls only that.\nPREDICTIONS (frozen):\n   H-M1 (primary, DEV): PPML beta_density < 0 (entries(t+1) on density(t)), concept-clustered 95% CI < 0.\n   H-M2 (primary, DEV): PPML beta_OPEN > 0 (entries(t+1) on OPEN_home(t)), 95% CI > 0.\n        Holm over {H-M1, H-M2}.\n   H-M3 (reverse path weaker): |std beta_fwd| - |std beta_rev| > 0 with a paired concept-bootstrap 95% CI > 0, where\n        the reverse is density(t+1) on entries(t) and the standardisation uses within-concept SDs.\n   H-M4 (event study): after the first closure jump, entries fall (mean of lags 0..+2 < 0, CI < 0); pre-trend joint Wald\n        test on leads -3..-1 has p > 0.10 AND max |lead| < 0.5 x |mean lag 0..+2|; the event-date permutation p < 0.05.\n   H-M5 (replication bodies): the signs of H-M1 and H-M2 hold on OLD_HELDOUT and COHORT (point estimates, CI reported).\n   VERDICT: MECHANISM SUPPORTED = H-M1 & H-M2 (Holm) & H-M3 & H-M4 & H-M5 signs;\n            PARTIAL = H-M1 or H-M2 holds but H-M3 or H-M4 fails (report which);\n            NOT SUPPORTED = both H-M1 and H-M2 CIs include 0 on DEV.\n   H-S1 (sequence): intersection-born concepts take off WITHOUT a prior home-prominence peak more often than single-home\n        concepts (share difference > 0, concept-bootstrap CI > 0).\n   H-P1 (partners, exploratory): method-topic and new-community partners carry more of the new_edge_rate signal (larger\n        psp with O2r_m50 given B5) than domain-topic and same-community partners.\n\n# ======================= 5a. TOPIC TYPING (LLM; parallel to Pass M; cap $1) =======================\ntopics = the distinct topic ids that appear as NEW partners in any concept's t0..t0+2 window, from EXP8\n   frame_matches_early (expected about 4.5k of 4,516 -> label all 4,516; still cheap)\nprompt (JSON output): topic display name + subfield + field + its 5 top keywords (from topic_meta if present, else name\n   only) -> {'class': 'METHOD' (technique, instrument, algorithm, analytic approach) | 'DOMAIN' (phenomenon, object,\n   organism, material, disease, place, population), 'confidence': 0-1}\nmodel A = a cheap OpenRouter model selected via aii-openrouter-llms by price (e.g. a Gemini Flash-Lite / GPT mini class\n   model); temperature 0; batches of 40 topics per call; asyncio + Semaphore(8); running cost from usage.cost; stop at $0.80\n   or on the first 'AI Inventor per-run OpenRouter budget' 403.\nBENCHMARK: 100 stratified topics double-labelled by model B (a different family); 40 hand-checked by the executor\n   (write the hand labels in results/topic_type_handcheck.csv BEFORE viewing the model labels).\n   Required: Cohen kappa(A, B) >= 0.6 AND accuracy vs hand >= 0.85. If this fails: revise the prompt once; if it fails\n   again, keep only topics where A == B and log it.\n-> WS/results/topic_types.csv (topic_id, name, field, class, confidence, model, agree_B)\n\n# ======================= 5b. PRIMARY FE ESTIMATION (about 45 min incl. bootstraps) =======================\npanel = yearly_features JOIN D3 (ci, t) -> outcome y(t+1) = entries(t+1), any_entry(t+1)\nsample rows: t0 <= t <= h_end - 1; at_risk(t) > 0; home-only deg(t) >= 2 (density defined); report the dropped share\nEstimator (pyfixest.fepois): y(t+1) ~ X(t) + log1p_home_works(t) + log1p_all_works(t) + log1p_deg(t) + log_at_risk(t)\n                                  | concept FE + calendar-year FE ; vcov = CRV1 by concept\n   X in {density, OPEN_home} as SEPARATE models (plus a joint model reported as secondary)\n   Concepts with all-zero y are dropped by PPML (report n).\n   NOTE: age = year - t0 is collinear with concept FE + year FE, so age is absorbed. Sensitivity S1 replaces year FE with\n   age FE (the linear trend is then absorbed instead).\n   NOTE: cum_entries(t-1) is NOT in the primary model (it is a lagged outcome; Nickell-type bias with T <= 11).\n   Sensitivity S2 adds it.\nBinary hazard: LPM with the same FE (pyfixest.feols) on any_entry(t+1); reported alongside.\nBootstrap: 2,000 concept-cluster resamples (resample concepts with replacement, relabel duplicates as new FE units,\n   refit) -> percentile 95% CI; joblib 6 workers. (With ~100k rows one fepois refit takes about 1-2 s -> about 10 min.)\nReverse path: density(t+1) ~ entries(t) + same controls at t+1 | concept FE + year FE (feols); standardise both betas by\n   within-concept SDs (the SD of the FE-demeaned variables); paired bootstrap of |std fwd| - |std rev| (same resampled\n   concepts in both).\nBodies: DEV (primary), OLD_HELDOUT, COHORT, each separately; per group within each body -> DL pooling with I2 over groups.\nRobustness (all pre-declared): dens_adj instead of density; exclude Medicine homes; exclude intersection-born; drop rows with\n   year >= 2015 (backbone clamp); ALL-PAPERS density instead of home-only (contrast: shows how much the all-papers version\n   is mechanically coupled); exclude rows where home coverage < 0.5; Poisson with log exposure = log at_risk as an offset\n   instead of a control.\n\n# ======================= 5c. EVENT STUDY (about 30 min) =======================\nwithin-concept SD of density over t0..h_end (needs >= 5 defined years)\nclosure jump at t* = the FIRST t with age >= 2 and density(t) - density(t-1) >= 1 within-concept SD (and deg(t) >= 3)\ncohort g = t*; never-treated = concepts with no jump; relative time e = t - g in -3..+4 (bins: <= -4 and >= +5 are\n   binned and not reported)\nSun & Abraham (2021) interaction-weighted estimator, implemented directly:\n   regress y(t+1) (and separately entries(t)) on sum_g sum_{e != -1} 1[G = g] x 1[t - g = e] + controls\n   | concept FE + year FE, with never-treated concepts as the control; then average the cohort-specific CATT(g, e) with\n   weights = the cohort shares among treated at each e. SEs by concept-cluster bootstrap (1,000).\n   Alternative control = the last-treated cohort (not-yet-treated); report both.\n   Cross-check: pyfixest event_study(estimator='saturated') if it exists in the installed version, else a statsmodels\n   OLS with the same dummies; the point estimates must agree to 1e-6.\nPre-trends: joint Wald test of leads -3..-2 (e = -1 omitted); also report Roth (2022)-style: the largest linear pre-trend\n   the test has 80% power to detect, given the lead SEs.\nPlacebos: (i) the event year is permuted within concept among eligible years (1,000 draws) -> the null distribution of the\n   mean lag 0..+2 effect; (ii) within-concept-year permutation of which off-home field was entered (keeps the counts,\n   breaks field identity; used on the partner-community check that entries move to fields outside the new partners'\n   communities).\nPlaceholders for mechanical checks: the same event study with home works(t) as the outcome (a closure jump should NOT\n   be simply a home-volume shock); report it.\n\n# ======================= 6. WHY IT WORKS: PARTNER-SOURCE DECOMPOSITION (early window t0..t0+2; about 30 min) ============\nfor each concept, the static new partners NEW = the EXP8 new-neighbour set over W1..W3 (all papers, the EXP8 definition,\n   recomputed by ego.concept_core with windows = rq1_windows; betweenness off) with, per new partner k:\n   type_k      = METHOD | DOMAIN (topic_types.csv)\n   pfield_k    = home | off-home (topic_meta field of k, mapped to the 26 venue-field codes; home_list)\n   comm_new_k  = comm[s(first_year_k)][k] != the concept's first-year modal community\n   carrier_k   = home | off-home | mixed (the venue fields of the concept papers in which k first appears)\nclass-restricted indicators: new_edge_rate_X = (|NEW & X| / 3) / (n1 + 1) and n_comm_W3_X = #communities among W3\n   neighbours in class X, for X in {METHOD, DOMAIN, pfield home, pfield off-home, comm_new, comm_old, carrier home,\n   carrier off-home}\nscore (EXPLORATORY; old held-out outcomes already unsealed): partial Spearman of each with O2r_m50 and O2r_resid given\n   B5 + group + t0 dummies (EXP8 rq1stats psp, identical code), 2,000 concept bootstraps; per body (DEV, OLD_HELDOUT,\n   COHORT) and DL-pooled over the 4 held-out groups with I2.\n   Paired bootstrap differences: METHOD - DOMAIN, comm_new - comm_old, carrier home - carrier off-home.\n   KEY QUESTION: does new_edge_rate restricted to partners delivered by HOME-venue papers keep psp > 0? If yes, the\n   signal is not a by-product of the concept already being in off-home venues.\nBRIDGING PAPERS: concept papers in t0..t0+2 that introduce >= 1 new partner from a community new to the concept.\n   Per concept: bridging share, and for bridging vs other early papers: median team size, share of authors new to the\n   concept (no earlier concept paper), review share (doc_type), home vs off-home venue share.\n   Test: psp(bridging_share, O2r_m50 | B5) per body.\n-> WS/results/partner_decomposition.json, WS/data/bridging_papers.parquet\n\n# ======================= 7. SEQUENCE TEST (RQ2; about 20 min) =======================\nprominence(ci, t) = the mean of the within-home percentile ranks of the home-only deg(t) and kcore(t), among all frame\n   concepts sharing the same primary home field in calendar year t (ranks with >= 20 concepts per home-year, else NA)\nprominence peak = argmax_t prominence over t0..h_end (ties -> earliest); peak must exceed the concept's median by >= 20\n   percentile points to count as a peak (else 'no peak')\ntake-off T = the first t with entries(t) >= 2 (off-home, D3); concepts without a take-off are censored\nTests:\n   (a) share with peak < T among concepts with a take-off: single-home vs intersection-born; concept bootstrap CI of the\n       difference (H-S1)\n   (b) time to take-off: Kaplan-Meier by single vs multi home; Cox model with group strata and log early volume\n   (c) two event studies with the Sun-Abraham code of 5c: outcome entries around the peak year; outcome prominence around T.\n       Pre-trends for both.\n   Report by body, and excluding Medicine homes.\n-> WS/results/sequence_tests.json\n\n# ======================= 8. FIGURES, AUDIT, OUTPUTS (about 30 min) =======================\nfigures (PNG + PDF, via aii-data-fig-gen house style):\n   fig_fe_coefs: forest plot of the standardised forward vs reverse betas, per body and group\n   fig_event_closure: event-study coefficients -3..+4 with 95% CIs (never-treated and not-yet-treated controls), plus the\n     placebo band\n   fig_partner_decomp: psp by partner class with CIs (DEV, old held-out)\n   fig_sequence: KM curves by single vs multi home + the prominence -> take-off event study\n   fig_panel_example: 4 matched concepts (Art 3 overlap allowed): yearly density, OPEN_home and entries\naudit.py (an independent code path): recompute H-M1 and H-M2 with statsmodels GLM Poisson + explicit concept and year\n   dummies on a 1,000-concept DEV subsample -> the same betas as pyfixest to 1e-5; recompute 3 Sun-Abraham cells by\n   hand; planted-effect and shuffled controls (T7).\nWrite: yearly_panel.parquet (features + outcomes, after the seal), fe_results.json, event_study.json, partner_decomposition.json,\n   topic_types.csv, topic_type_benchmark.json, bridging_papers.parquet, sequence_tests.json, frozen_spec.json + logs/seal.log,\n   deviations.json, provenance.json, pipeline_counts.json (works, concept-years, rows at each filter, events, per body) for\n   the paper's methodology figure, and method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out): one example per concept-year row of the\n   estimation sample; input = the X(t) vector and controls; output = entries(t+1); predict_fe_density / predict_fe_open /\n   predict_controls_only = fitted values from the three models (DEV fitted in 5 concept folds, out-of-fold);\n   metadata = ci, body, group, t, age. Full/mini/preview via aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if > limit.\nREADME.md: layout, how to run, the verdict table (H-M1..H-M5, H-S1, H-P1 with the deciding quantities), the deviations,\n   and 'Restoring removed files'.\n.aii/manifest.yaml: keep data/yearly_panel.parquet, data/yearly_features.parquet, data/frame_matches_long/ (regenerable but\n   costly), results/, figures/; delete .venv/, passM/parts/, __pycache__/.",

All files are < the size limit (aii-file-size-limit). OpenRouter spend is recorded in results/llm_cost.json and is <= $1.",
  "domain_practice": "WHAT I READ for this plan: the iteration-4 strategy (gen_strat_1 struct_out), the run report current_report.md (iterations 1-3), and on disk the code this plan reuses: EXP8 lib/ego.py (concept_core, windows, density and persistence definitions), EXP8 passA.py (the snapshot pass and its 62-minute runtime), the EXP8 README runtime table, EXP7 method.py state_panel() and lib/h2_exp6.states(), and the EXP5 frame_concepts.csv schema. No new literature search was run in this planning session: its prompt arrived truncated, and the novelty check is the sibling Research artifact's job. The econometric practice below is the standard panel/event-study canon the strategy itself cites (Sun & Abraham 2021; Callaway & Sant'Anna 2021) plus well-established companions. The citations are to be verified with aii-semscholar-bib at write-up.\n\nWITHIN-UNIT TIMING DESIGNS. In science-of-science and diffusion work, a claim that X precedes Y within units is expected to use unit and time fixed effects, a count model suited to many zeros, and an explicit reverse-path or lead test. The usual count model is Poisson pseudo-maximum likelihood with high-dimensional FE (Santos Silva & Tenreyro 2006; Wooldridge 1999 on the consistency of FE Poisson under only a correct conditional mean; Correia, Guimaraes & Zylkin 2020 ppmlhdfe; pyfixest fepois in Python). Linear probability models on binary hazards are the robustness twin. Lagged outcomes in short FE panels create Nickell (1981) bias, so the cumulative-entries control is kept out of the primary model.\n\nSTAGGERED EVENT STUDIES. Since 2020, two-way-FE event studies with staggered timing and heterogeneous effects are known to be biased: already-treated units act as controls with negative weights (Goodman-Bacon 2021; de Chaisemartin & D'Haultfoeuille 2020; Sun & Abraham 2021; Callaway & Sant'Anna 2021; Borusyak, Jaravel & Spiess 2024). Reviewers now expect a heterogeneity-robust estimator, never-treated or not-yet-treated controls stated explicitly, and pre-trend tests. They also expect awareness that pre-trend tests have low power: Roth (2022) recommends reporting what violation the test could detect.\n\nCONTROLS SPECIFIC TO EGO-NETWORK MEASURES. Ego-network density falls mechanically with ego size, so density comparisons need a size control or a size-matched null. The configuration/degree-matched null is standard in network science (e.g. Maslov & Sneppen 2002; the structural-diversity literature Ugander et al. 2012 conditions on neighbourhood size). In co-occurrence emergence work, the 'density vs centrality' strategic diagram (Callon, Courtial & Laville 1991) is the classic lens. Because density there is a THEME property, a 'closure' measure must separate a genuine tightening of the neighbourhood from simply having fewer papers. Hence the home-volume controls, the log degree, the dens_adj null and the home-volume event-study check.\n\nMECHANICAL COUPLING. A diffusion outcome and a network indicator built from the same papers are coupled. The standard remedy is to build the predictor from a disjoint set: here, home-venue papers predict off-home venue entries. The ALL-PAPERS build is kept only as the demonstration of that coupling.\n\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Panel studies of topic or field dynamics typically have thousands of units and 10-20 periods. Here there are about 12.5k concepts x up to 11 years (about 100-120k usable rows), which is ample for FE Poisson. The binding constraint is the number of closure-jump events per cohort in the event study, which is checked before the seal (F6). Effects on record for static openness are small (partial rho about 0.08-0.17), so within-concept effects of a similar standardised size (about 3-10% change in the entry rate per within-concept SD) are the realistic target. CIs, not p-values alone, are reported, with per-group results and DL-pooled I2 as in the run's practice.\n\nMEASUREMENT DETAIL. Topic typing by LLM needs a benchmark: two-model agreement plus hand checks, reported with kappa, as is now expected for LLM annotation in computational social science. OpenAlex topics (2024 taxonomy, back-assigned) are a known anachronism for early years. The run's EXP3 backbone uses them throughout, so the anachronism is shared by features and background and is stated as a limitation.",
  "practice_alignment": "MEETS:\n(1) Concept and calendar-year FE with PPML for the count outcome, an LPM twin for the binary hazard, concept-clustered CIs and a 2,000-draw concept-cluster refit bootstrap.\n(2) The reverse path is estimated with the same FE and compared on a within-concept standardised scale with a paired bootstrap, so 'openness leads spread' is tested against 'spread leads openness'.\n(3) A heterogeneity-robust Sun-Abraham event study with never-treated and not-yet-treated controls, a pre-trend joint test plus a Roth-style power statement, and two permutation placebos. A plain TWFE event study is shown only in the simulation unit test to demonstrate its bias.\n(4) Ego-density size confounding is handled by a log-degree control and a degree-matched null (dens_adj) robustness, and the home-volume shock check keeps a 'closure jump' from being a volume dip.\n(5) Mechanical coupling is removed by construction: home-venue papers build the predictor, off-home D3 entries are the outcome. The ALL-PAPERS build is reported only as a contrast.\n(6) Nickell bias is avoided by keeping the lagged cumulative outcome out of the primary model (it enters only in sensitivity S2). The age/year/concept collinearity is stated and handled by swapping the FE (S1).\n(7) The pre-registration is sealed before outcomes are attached, with explicit verdict rules, Holm over the two primary tests, and per-body and per-group reporting with DL pooling and I2.\n(8) LLM topic typing is benchmarked (two-model kappa and hand accuracy) with a pre-declared lexical fallback.\n\nDEPARTURES AND THEIR COST:\n(a) The outcomes are not virgin. EXP7/EXP8 already examined D3 states and static breadth for these concepts, so the seal protects only the new within-concept estimand. Cost: this is mechanism evidence, not confirmation. It is labelled as such, as the strategy requires, and the sibling Art 1 carries the confirmatory burden.\n(b) Yearly z constants for OPEN_home are frozen here on DEV concept-year features rather than taken from Art 1's static constants, because Art 1 runs in parallel and the yearly components are on a different scale. Cost: OPEN_home(t) is not numerically identical to Art 1's OPEN; the sign convention and components are the same.\n(c) Years >= 2015 use the 2010-14 backbone slice (the EXP3 backbone ends in 2014). Cost: community labels for late years are slightly stale. There is a sensitivity dropping those rows.\n(d) 1-year windows make yearly ego statistics noisy for small concepts. The F4 threshold rule, decided on features before the seal, trades noise against coverage.\n(e) Causal language is limited. Concept FE absorb time-invariant confounders (type, footprint, home field), but time-varying shocks (e.g. a field-wide funding surge that raises home density and lowers off-home entry) are not ruled out. Year FE absorb only common shocks. A home-field x year FE sensitivity is feasible and is added to the robustness list if runtime allows (it is the first item after the F8 drop list). The paper will say 'precedes and predicts within concept', not 'causes'.\n(f) Callaway & Sant'Anna is not run separately. Sun-Abraham with never-treated controls identifies the same cohort-time ATTs under the same parallel-trends assumption, and a second estimator would cost time with little extra information. A reviewer may still ask for it, and the code layout permits adding it in iteration 5.\n(g) The partner-decomposition psp values are exploratory, on old held-out outcomes already unsealed. They explain the static signal; they do not re-confirm it."
}
