SETUP (about 15 min) =======================\\n# Read the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-use-hardware, aii-json, aii-file-size-limit\\n# and aii-openrouter-llms first. WS = this workspace; R = run root. Every write goes under WS. The OpenAlex API is NOT used\\n# (0 credits); only the public S3 snapshot via HTTP range reads.\\nuv venv; uv pip install numpy pandas pyarrow scipy scikit-learn statsmodels pyfixest python-igraph leidenalg loguru\\n   matplotlib pyahocorasick nltk joblib\\n# Copy (do not import across workspaces) into WS/lib/, recording sha256 per copy in WS/results/provenance.json:\\n#   EXP8: passA.py -> passM.py, lib/{matcher,rangefile,common,common5,common3,ego,ego_ctx,stats_core,seal}.py\\n#   EXP7: lib/d3.py, lib/h2_exp6.py\\n#   EXP3: backbone/slice*.npz, scan/topic_ids.json, results/topic_meta.csv\\n#   EXP5: lexicon_v1.parquet (check the sha against frozen_lexicon.sha256), results/source_field.parquet,\\n#         snapshot/works_manifest.json\\nframe = read_csv(EXP5/frame_concepts.csv)   # assert 12,499 rows; split x group counts == {DEV 4771, PHYS 742,\\n                                             # LIFEENV 1113, SOC 1352, MATHDEC 165, COHORT 4356}\\nframe['home_list'] = [int(h) for h in home.split('|')]; frame['multi_home'] = intersect40 == 1\\nframe['body'] = DEV | OLD_HELDOUT (split == HELDOUT) | COHORT\\nframe['h_end'] = min(t0 + 10, 2022)        # COHORT concepts have shorter panels (t0 2010-14 -> 8-12 years); logged\\n\\n# ======================= 1. PASS M: one zero-credit snapshot pass (about 70-90 min, in the BACKGROUND) =======================\\n# Adapt EXP8 passA.process_file. The base filter, automaton, stemmed verification, TAG_MIN = 0.3 and venue lookup are\\n# UNCHANGED. Changes:\\n#   (i) title matching for publication years 2000..2022 (was 2000..2016)\\n#   (ii) keep rows for frame hits with tagstate == 1 and t0 - 3 <= year <= min(t0 + 10, 2022)\\n#   (iii) add the column 'type' (article | review) to the emitted row\\n#   (iv) authors for ALL kept years (was year >= t0 only); cited_by_count is dropped (not needed)\\n#   (v) BG/GT are NOT recomputed; reuse EXP8 data/bg_topics.npz (1995-2022, same filter) -> faster\\nCOLS_M = passA.COLS minus 'cited_by_count' plus 'type'\\nrow = (ci int32, year int16, work_id int64, vfield int8, doc_type int8 {0 article, 1 review}, topic_idx list[int16],\\n       author_ids list[int64])\\nwrite WS/passM/parts/part_{fi}.parquet + done_{fi}.json (resumable); run with ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers),\\n   8 range-read threads per worker; PID file + nohup; poll the log every 5 min\\nmerge -> WS/data/frame_matches_long/part_*.parquet (each < 90 MB). Expected: 12.5k concepts x about 600 works = about\\n   7-8M rows, about 0.6-1.0 GB of parquet -> kept as parts; aii-file-size-limit applies.\\nCHECK M1 (STOP rule): per concept, grounded counts per (year, vfield) from Pass M must EQUAL EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1)\\n   for every year <= 2022, for >= 99.5% of concept-years; the median per-concept Spearman over years must be >= 0.999.\\n   Otherwise STOP, diagnose (lexicon sha, automaton, year window, base filter) and rerun on 5 files before continuing.\\nCHECK M2: for t0-3..t0+2 the (ci, work_id) set equals EXP8 frame_matches_early for >= 99.5% of rows, and the topic lists\\n   are identical.\\n# While Pass M runs: write steps 2-4 and the unit tests; run the topic typing (step 5a), which needs no pass.\\n\\n# ======================= 2. D3 STATE PANEL (about 10 min) =======================\\nsp = concat(EXP7 state_panel_dev.parquet, state_panel_heldout.parquet)\\nmissing = frame concepts absent from sp (EXP7's DEV panel may be 'EXP5 minus EXP6'; log the count)\\nfor missing concepts: G = agg_counts (tagstate == 1) -> [NY, 27] per concept; states via d3.panel_states / h2_exp6.states\\n   (min_n = 2), the SAME code; VALIDATE by recomputing 200 random concepts already in sp: every cell must be equal.\\nPer concept-year t, over off-home fields:\\n   entries(t)   = #fields whose state switches to 'entered' (cum reaches 2) in year t\\n   any_entry(t) = entries(t) > 0\\n   at_risk(t)   = #off-home fields still untouched at the end of t-1  (the risk-set size; 26 - |home| - already entered)\\n   cum_entries(t-1), retained(t), lost(t)\\n\\n# ======================= 3. YEARLY HOME-ONLY EGO PANEL (features only; about 40 min on 6 workers) =======================\\n# A new function ego_year(ci, t) in WS/lib/ego_yearly.py, built from ego.py primitives (neighbours, pmi, bg_window,\\n# window_counts, self_topics). NO betweenness. Context via ego_ctx with EXP8 bg_topics.npz.\\nworks_home(ci)  = [(year, topic_idx)] for grounded works with vfield in home_list (vfield == 0 unlabelled -> excluded;\\n                  log home-paper coverage = home works / all grounded works per concept-year)\\nworks_all(ci)   = all grounded works (for the ALL-PAPERS comparison build)\\nSELF(ci) = ego.self_topics(name, aliases, counts over t0..t0+2 of works_all)   # frozen once per concept, as in EXP8\\ns(t) = slice_of(t) using EXP3 SLICES; years >= 2015 clamp to slice 2 (2010-14) -> logged; sensitivity drops those rows\\nfor t in t0..h_end:\\n   NB(t)   = neighbours(cnt_home[t], nc_home[t], bg[t], GT[t], SELF, min_n = 2)    # 1-year window, as EXP8 W1..W3\\n   SEEN(t) = union of topics with >= 1 home count in t0-3..t-1\\n   NEW(t)  = NB(t) & ~SEEN(t)\\n   new_rate(t)   = |NEW(t)| / (|NB(t-1)| + 1)                                      # yearly analogue of new_edge_rate\\n   n_comm(t)     = #distinct comm[s(t)] labels among NB(t)\\n   participation(t) = 1 - sum_c w_c^2, with w_c = count-weighted share of NB(t) in community c\\n   nov_res(t)    = share of NEW(t) outside the concept's first-year (t0) modal community, minus the degree-weighted\\n                   expectation over the pool (EXP8 NOV_res formula, applied to NEW(t))\\n   density(t)    = (#full_edges[s(t)] among NB(t)) / C(|NB(t)|, 2)      (NA if |NB(t)| < 2)\\n   dens_null(t)  = mean density of 100 random topic sets of size |NB(t)| drawn with bg[t]-proportional weights from the\\n                   non-SELF pool (Gumbel top-k as in ego.distinct_null); dens_adj(t) = density(t) - dens_null(t)\\n   persistence(t)= Jaccard(NB(t-1), NB(t))\\n   deg(t) = |NB(t)|, kcore(t) = coreness of the concept node inserted into the kNN graph of s(t) (igraph coreness only)\\n   controls: log1p home works(t), log1p all works(t), log1p deg(t), log(at_risk(t)), concept age a = t - t0\\n   ALSO for works_all: density_all(t), new_rate_all(t) (the ALL-PAPERS build, for comparison only)\\nOPEN_home(t) = mean of z(new_rate), z(n_comm), z(participation), z(nov_res), -z(density), -z(persistence); needs >= 4 of 6.\\n   The z constants are the mean and SD over DEV concept-years, computed from FEATURE columns only and frozen in step 4.\\n   (DEVIATION: the strategy asked for Art 1's EXP5 static constants. Art 1 runs in parallel and the yearly components\\n   have a different scale, so own constants are frozen here and logged.)\\nParallelise over concepts: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers), context loaded once per worker; chunks of 200 concepts;\\n   checkpoint per chunk. Timing test on 100 concepts first (T4) -> extrapolate.\\nWRITE WS/data/yearly_features.parquet (no outcome columns).\\nPORT CHECK: aggregating ego_year over t0..t0+2 with EXP8 definitions (windows W1..W3 = t0, t0+1, t0+2, all papers)\\n   must reproduce EXP8 ego_features new_edge_rate, n_comm_W3, participation, ego_density_W3, edge_persistence to 1e-9 on\\n   300 random concepts. Otherwise STOP and fix the port.\\n\\n# ======================= 4. PRE-REGISTRATION AND SEAL (before any outcome join) =======================\\nWS/prereg.md + WS/results/frozen_spec.json:\\n   panel definition, the yearly formulas, the yearly z constants, the closure-jump rule, the take-off rule, the prominence\\n   definition, the estimators, controls, FE, clustering, bootstrap B and seed (20260929), Holm families, the predictions\\n   and verdict rules below, the sha256 of every WS/lib/*.py file and of yearly_features.parquet.\\n   sha256(frozen_spec.json) -> WS/logs/seal.log; git commit. ONLY THEN does the estimation script attach D3 outcomes\\n   (lib/seal.py gate: attach_outcomes() raises unless seal.log matches frozen_spec).\\n   HONEST NOTE in prereg.md: EXP7/EXP8 already looked at D3 states and static breadth for these concepts. The within-\\n   concept yearly estimand was never estimated, and this seal controls only that.\\nPREDICTIONS (frozen):\\n   H-M1 (primary, DEV): PPML beta_density < 0 (entries(t+1) on density(t)), concept-clustered 95% CI < 0.\\n   H-M2 (primary, DEV): PPML beta_OPEN > 0 (entries(t+1) on OPEN_home(t)), 95% CI > 0.\\n        Holm over {H-M1, H-M2}.\\n   H-M3 (reverse path weaker): |std beta_fwd| - |std beta_rev| > 0 with a paired concept-bootstrap 95% CI > 0, where\\n        the reverse is density(t+1) on entries(t) and the standardisation uses within-concept SDs.\\n   H-M4 (event study): after the first closure jump, entries fall (mean of lags 0..+2 < 0, CI < 0); pre-trend joint Wald\\n        test on leads -3..-1 has p > 0.10 AND max |lead| < 0.5 x |mean lag 0..+2|; the event-date permutation p < 0.05.\\n   H-M5 (replication bodies): the signs of H-M1 and H-M2 hold on OLD_HELDOUT and COHORT (point estimates, CI reported).\\n   VERDICT: MECHANISM SUPPORTED = H-M1 & H-M2 (Holm) & H-M3 & H-M4 & H-M5 signs;\\n            PARTIAL = H-M1 or H-M2 holds but H-M3 or H-M4 fails (report which);\\n            NOT SUPPORTED = both H-M1 and H-M2 CIs include 0 on DEV.\\n   H-S1 (sequence): intersection-born concepts take off WITHOUT a prior home-prominence peak more often than single-home\\n        concepts (share difference > 0, concept-bootstrap CI > 0).\\n   H-P1 (partners, exploratory): method-topic and new-community partners carry more of the new_edge_rate signal (larger\\n        psp with O2r_m50 given B5) than domain-topic and same-community partners.\\n\\n# ======================= 5a. TOPIC TYPING (LLM; parallel to Pass M; cap $1) =======================\\ntopics = the distinct topic ids that appear as NEW partners in any concept's t0..t0+2 window, from EXP8\\n   frame_matches_early (expected about 4.5k of 4,516 -> label all 4,516; still cheap)\\nprompt (JSON output): topic display name + subfield + field + its 5 top keywords (from topic_meta if present, else name\\n   only) -> {'class': 'METHOD' (technique, instrument, algorithm, analytic approach) | 'DOMAIN' (phenomenon, object,\\n   organism, material, disease, place, population), 'confidence': 0-1}\\nmodel A = a cheap OpenRouter model selected via aii-openrouter-llms by price (e.g. a Gemini Flash-Lite / GPT mini class\\n   model); temperature 0; batches of 40 topics per call; asyncio + Semaphore(8); running cost from usage.cost; stop at $0.80\\n   or on the first 'AI Inventor per-run OpenRouter budget' 403.\\nBENCHMARK: 100 stratified topics double-labelled by model B (a different family); 40 hand-checked by the executor\\n   (write the hand labels in results/topic_type_handcheck.csv BEFORE viewing the model labels).\\n   Required: Cohen kappa(A, B) >= 0.6 AND accuracy vs hand >= 0.85. If this fails: revise the prompt once; if it fails\\n   again, keep only topics where A == B and log it.\\n-> WS/results/topic_types.csv (topic_id, name, field, class, confidence, model, agree_B)\\n\\n# ======================= 5b. PRIMARY FE ESTIMATION (about 45 min incl. bootstraps) =======================\\npanel = yearly_features JOIN D3 (ci, t) -> outcome y(t+1) = entries(t+1), any_entry(t+1)\\nsample rows: t0 <= t <= h_end - 1; at_risk(t) > 0; home-only deg(t) >= 2 (density defined); report the dropped share\\nEstimator (pyfixest.fepois): y(t+1) ~ X(t) + log1p_home_works(t) + log1p_all_works(t) + log1p_deg(t) + log_at_risk(t)\\n                                  | concept FE + calendar-year FE ; vcov = CRV1 by concept\\n   X in {density, OPEN_home} as SEPARATE models (plus a joint model reported as secondary)\\n   Concepts with all-zero y are dropped by PPML (report n).\\n   NOTE: age = year - t0 is collinear with concept FE + year FE, so age is absorbed. Sensitivity S1 replaces year FE with\\n   age FE (the linear trend is then absorbed instead).\\n   NOTE: cum_entries(t-1) is NOT in the primary model (it is a lagged outcome; Nickell-type bias with T <= 11).\\n   Sensitivity S2 adds it.\\nBinary hazard: LPM with the same FE (pyfixest.feols) on any_entry(t+1); reported alongside.\\nBootstrap: 2,000 concept-cluster resamples (resample concepts with replacement, relabel duplicates as new FE units,\\n   refit) -> percentile 95% CI; joblib 6 workers. (With ~100k rows one fepois refit takes about 1-2 s -> about 10 min.)\\nReverse path: density(t+1) ~ entries(t) + same controls at t+1 | concept FE + year FE (feols); standardise both betas by\\n   within-concept SDs (the SD of the FE-demeaned variables); paired bootstrap of |std fwd| - |std rev| (same resampled\\n   concepts in both).\\nBodies: DEV (primary), OLD_HELDOUT, COHORT, each separately; per group within each body -> DL pooling with I2 over groups.\\nRobustness (all pre-declared): dens_adj instead of density; exclude Medicine homes; exclude intersection-born; drop rows with\\n   year >= 2015 (backbone clamp); ALL-PAPERS density instead of home-only (contrast: shows how much the all-papers version\\n   is mechanically coupled); exclude rows where home coverage < 0.5; Poisson with log exposure = log at_risk as an offset\\n   instead of a control.\\n\\n# ======================= 5c. EVENT STUDY (about 30 min) =======================\\nwithin-concept SD of density over t0..h_end (needs >= 5 defined years)\\nclosure jump at t* = the FIRST t with age >= 2 and density(t) - density(t-1) >= 1 within-concept SD (and deg(t) >= 3)\\ncohort g = t*; never-treated = concepts with no jump; relative time e = t - g in -3..+4 (bins: <= -4 and >= +5 are\\n   binned and not reported)\\nSun & Abraham (2021) interaction-weighted estimator, implemented directly:\\n   regress y(t+1) (and separately entries(t)) on sum_g sum_{e != -1} 1[G = g] x 1[t - g = e] + controls\\n   | concept FE + year FE, with never-treated concepts as the control; then average the cohort-specific CATT(g, e) with\\n   weights = the cohort shares among treated at each e. SEs by concept-cluster bootstrap (1,000).\\n   Alternative control = the last-treated cohort (not-yet-treated); report both.\\n   Cross-check: pyfixest event_study(estimator='saturated') if it exists in the installed version, else a statsmodels\\n   OLS with the same dummies; the point estimates must agree to 1e-6.\\nPre-trends: joint Wald test of leads -3..-2 (e = -1 omitted); also report Roth (2022)-style: the largest linear pre-trend\\n   the test has 80% power to detect, given the lead SEs.\\nPlacebos: (i) the event year is permuted within concept among eligible years (1,000 draws) -> the null distribution of the\\n   mean lag 0..+2 effect; (ii) within-concept-year permutation of which off-home field was entered (keeps the counts,\\n   breaks field identity; used on the partner-community check that entries move to fields outside the new partners'\\n   communities).\\nPlaceholders for mechanical checks: the same event study with home works(t) as the outcome (a closure jump should NOT\\n   be simply a home-volume shock); report it.\\n\\n# ======================= 6. WHY IT WORKS: PARTNER-SOURCE DECOMPOSITION (early window t0..t0+2; about 30 min) ============\\nfor each concept, the static new partners NEW = the EXP8 new-neighbour set over W1..W3 (all papers, the EXP8 definition,\\n   recomputed by ego.concept_core with windows = rq1_windows; betweenness off) with, per new partner k:\\n   type_k      = METHOD | DOMAIN (topic_types.csv)\\n   pfield_k    = home | off-home (topic_meta field of k, mapped to the 26 venue-field codes; home_list)\\n   comm_new_k  = comm[s(first_year_k)][k] != the concept's first-year modal community\\n   carrier_k   = home | off-home | mixed (the venue fields of the concept papers in which k first appears)\\nclass-restricted indicators: new_edge_rate_X = (|NEW & X| / 3) / (n1 + 1) and n_comm_W3_X = #communities among W3\\n   neighbours in class X, for X in {METHOD, DOMAIN, pfield home, pfield off-home, comm_new, comm_old, carrier home,\\n   carrier off-home}\\nscore (EXPLORATORY; old held-out outcomes already unsealed): partial Spearman of each with O2r_m50 and O2r_resid given\\n   B5 + group + t0 dummies (EXP8 rq1stats psp, identical code), 2,000 concept bootstraps; per body (DEV, OLD_HELDOUT,\\n   COHORT) and DL-pooled over the 4 held-out groups with I2.\\n   Paired bootstrap differences: METHOD - DOMAIN, comm_new - comm_old, carrier home - carrier off-home.\\n   KEY QUESTION: does new_edge_rate restricted to partners delivered by HOME-venue papers keep psp > 0? If yes, the\\n   signal is not a by-product of the concept already being in off-home venues.\\nBRIDGING PAPERS: concept papers in t0..t0+2 that introduce >= 1 new partner from a community new to the concept.\\n   Per concept: bridging share, and for bridging vs other early papers: median team size, share of authors new to the\\n   concept (no earlier concept paper), review share (doc_type), home vs off-home venue share.\\n   Test: psp(bridging_share, O2r_m50 | B5) per body.\\n-> WS/results/partner_decomposition.json, WS/data/bridging_papers.parquet\\n\\n# ======================= 7. SEQUENCE TEST (RQ2; about 20 min) =======================\\nprominence(ci, t) = the mean of the within-home percentile ranks of the home-only deg(t) and kcore(t), among all frame\\n   concepts sharing the same primary home field in calendar year t (ranks with >= 20 concepts per home-year, else NA)\\nprominence peak = argmax_t prominence over t0..h_end (ties -> earliest); peak must exceed the concept's median by >= 20\\n   percentile points to count as a peak (else 'no peak')\\ntake-off T = the first t with entries(t) >= 2 (off-home, D3); concepts without a take-off are censored\\nTests:\\n   (a) share with peak < T among concepts with a take-off: single-home vs intersection-born; concept bootstrap CI of the\\n       difference (H-S1)\\n   (b) time to take-off: Kaplan-Meier by single vs multi home; Cox model with group strata and log early volume\\n   (c) two event studies with the Sun-Abraham code of 5c: outcome entries around the peak year; outcome prominence around T.\\n       Pre-trends for both.\\n   Report by body, and excluding Medicine homes.\\n-> WS/results/sequence_tests.json\\n\\n# ======================= 8. FIGURES, AUDIT, OUTPUTS (about 30 min) =======================\\nfigures (PNG + PDF, via aii-data-fig-gen house style):\\n   fig_fe_coefs: forest plot of the standardised forward vs reverse betas, per body and group\\n   fig_event_closure: event-study coefficients -3..+4 with 95% CIs (never-treated and not-yet-treated controls), plus the\\n     placebo band\\n   fig_partner_decomp: psp by partner class with CIs (DEV, old held-out)\\n   fig_sequence: KM curves by single vs multi home + the prominence -> take-off event study\\n   fig_panel_example: 4 matched concepts (Art 3 overlap allowed): yearly density, OPEN_home and entries\\naudit.py (an independent code path): recompute H-M1 and H-M2 with statsmodels GLM Poisson + explicit concept and year\\n   dummies on a 1,000-concept DEV subsample -> the same betas as pyfixest to 1e-5; recompute 3 Sun-Abraham cells by\\n   hand; planted-effect and shuffled controls (T7).\\nWrite: yearly_panel.parquet (features + outcomes, after the seal), fe_results.json, event_study.json, partner_decomposition.json,\\n   topic_types.csv, topic_type_benchmark.json, bridging_papers.parquet, sequence_tests.json, frozen_spec.json + logs/seal.log,\\n   deviations.json, provenance.json, pipeline_counts.json (works, concept-years, rows at each filter, events, per body) for\\n   the paper's methodology figure, and method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out): one example per concept-year row of the\\n   estimation sample; input = the X(t) vector and controls; output = entries(t+1); predict_fe_density / predict_fe_open /\\n   predict_controls_only = fitted values from the three models (DEV fitted in 5 concept folds, out-of-fold);\\n   metadata = ci, body, group, t, age. Full/mini/preview via aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if > limit.\\nREADME.md: layout, how to run, the verdict table (H-M1..H-M5, H-S1, H-P1 with the deciding quantities), the deviations,\\n   and 'Restoring removed files'.\\n.aii/manifest.yaml: keep data/yearly_panel.parquet, data/yearly_features.parquet, data/frame_matches_long/ (regenerable but\\n   costly), results/, figures/; delete .venv/, passM/parts/, __pycache__/.\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"F1 SNAPSHOT SLOW OR UNREACHABLE (Pass M projected > 120 min after 40 files, or S3 errors):\\n(a) First drop author ids from COLS_M. Bridging-paper 'new authors' and team size are then lost; everything else stays.\\n(b) If still too slow, restrict title matching to years t0+3..t0+10, the only years EXP8 lacks, and reuse EXP8 frame_matches_early for t0-3..t0+2. This saves the early years, but authors before t0 stay missing (log it).\\n(c) If S3 is entirely unreachable: the mechanism panel falls back to FIELD-level yearly home-only proxies from EXP5 agg_counts (ptfield x vfield). Home-only 'neighbours' = primary-topic fields of home-venue works; density on the 26-field phi backbone; new-field-partner rate; persistence. Run the same FE and event-study pipeline, and label the result 'coarse field-level proxy; topic-level ego network not recomputed'. The partner decomposition then runs on the EXP8 early window only (frame_matches_early has topics and authors), so step 6 is fully unaffected.\\n\\nF2 CHECK M1 or M2 FAILS: never proceed with an unreproduced panel. Diagnose in this order: lexicon sha, full-lexicon automaton, year window, base-type filter, TAG_MIN, venue LUT. Rerun on 5 files. If a residual mismatch is < 1% of concept-years and explained (e.g. works whose publication_year changed between snapshot reads), document it and drop the affected concept-years.\\n\\nF3 YEARLY PORT CHECK FAILS (> 1e-9 vs EXP8 static features): the yearly code must be fixed first. As a last resort, compute the yearly features by calling ego.concept_core-style code on sliding 1-year windows and derive only the components that match. Log which components are dropped from OPEN_home; the >= 4 of 6 rule is kept.\\n\\nF4 TOO FEW DEFINED ROWS: if home-only deg(t) >= 2 holds for < 40% of concept-years, lower the neighbour threshold min_n from 2 to 1 for the yearly windows only, decided on DEV FEATURE distributions before the seal (no outcomes). If still < 40%, use 2-year rolling windows {t-1, t} and log that adjacent rows overlap. The clustered bootstrap already handles the dependence.\\n\\nF5 PPML CONVERGENCE OR SEPARATION problems (pyfixest warnings, > 20% of concepts dropped for all-zero outcomes): report the dropped concepts. Switch the primary to the LPM on any_entry(t+1), with the Poisson as secondary, and note it as a deviation. If pyfixest is unavailable, use statsmodels Poisson on within-transformed data (a Hausman-Wooldridge conditional Poisson via GLM with concept dummies on 5k-concept chunks) and log it.\\n\\nF6 TOO FEW CLOSURE-JUMP EVENTS (< 300 treated concepts in DEV): relax the jump to >= 0.75 within-concept SD (pre-declared as a second rung), else report the event study as underpowered with the Roth pre-trend power numbers and rely on the FE estimates.\\n\\nF7 LLM TOPIC TYPING FAILS THE BENCHMARK TWICE, OR THE BUDGET IS EXHAUSTED: use a deterministic lexical rule on topic names as the METHOD flag ('method', 'technique', 'algorithm', 'analysis', 'model', 'imaging', 'spectroscopy', 'sequencing', 'learning', 'estimation', 'assay', 'simulation', 'microscopy', 'optimization', 'detection', 'measurement'), hand-check 40 topics, and report the result as a lexical proxy.\\n\\nF8 TIME PRESSURE: the drop order is (1) ALL-PAPERS comparison build, (2) sequence event studies (keep the KM/Cox and share test), (3) bridging-paper profile, (4) the not-yet-treated event-study variant, (5) COHORT body. Never drop: the seal before the outcome join, the primary FE models with the concept bootstrap, the reverse path, the closure event study with pre-trends and the event-date placebo, the method-vs-domain partner split, the README verdict table, or method_out.json validation.\\n\\nF9 PREMATURE FORMAT FAILURE (the iteration-3 Exp9 loss): write a skeleton method_out.json with 3 fake rows and validate it with aii-json against exp_gen_sol_out in the first 20 minutes. Re-validate the MINI output after every stage.\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"T0 UNIT TESTS (no network, < 5 min; results/unit_tests.json).\\n(1) ego_year on a hand-built 6-topic toy backbone (known full edges, communities, bg): NB, NEW, new_rate, n_comm, participation, density and persistence equal hand values.\\n(2) dens_null: for a complete graph the null density is 1; for an empty graph it is 0; for a random graph with p = 0.3 the mean over 2,000 draws is within 0.01 of 0.3.\\n(3) D3 helpers: entries(t), at_risk(t) and any_entry on a hand-made 5-field x 8-year count matrix match hand values, and match h2_exp6.states().\\n(4) PPML with concept + year FE on simulated data (2,000 concepts x 10 years, y ~ Poisson(exp(a_c + d_t + 0.3 x))): beta within 0.02 of 0.3 in the mean over 50 simulations. With x independent of y: mean |beta| < 0.01, and the 95% CI covers 0 in 93-97% of simulations (clustered-bootstrap calibration).\\n(5) Sun-Abraham on simulated staggered adoption with HETEROGENEOUS effects by cohort (true ATT(e) known): the IW estimator recovers ATT(e) within 0.02, while the plain two-way FE event study is biased. This proves the implementation handles heterogeneity.\\n(6) Reverse-path paired bootstrap: with a simulated one-directional effect x(t) -> y(t+1), H-M3 holds in >= 90% of simulations; with symmetric feedback it holds in <= 10%.\\n(7) The seal gate raises if frozen_spec is missing or its hash mismatches, and raises on a second outcome attach.\\n(8) psp: this must be the SAME function as EXP8 rq1stats (import the copied file) and reproduce one EXP8 held-out psp (e.g. new_edge_rate pooled +0.118) to 1e-6 from the EXP8 arrays.\\n\\nT1 PASS M MINI (files 65, 1125, 1407, 1918, the EXP8 stage files): per-file runtime extrapolated over 2,040 files at 6 workers must be <= 110 min, else F1(a). Grounded (ci, year, vfield) counts on these files equal the EXP5 stage-test aggregates exactly. The early-window rows equal the EXP8 per-file rows (results/t1_passA_exact_65_1125_1407_1918.json is the reference). Unknown topic ids < 1%. doc_type is non-null for 100% of rows.\\n\\nT2 PASS M FULL: checks M1 and M2 with their numbers written to results/checks.json.\\n\\nT3 D3 VALIDATION: 200 random concepts recomputed from agg_counts equal EXP7 state_panel cell-for-cell. All files are < the size limit (aii-file-size-limit). OpenRouter spend is recorded in results/llm_cost.json and is <= $1.\",\n  \"domain_practice\": \"WHAT I READ for this plan: the iteration-4 strategy (gen_strat_1 struct_out), the run report current_report.md (iterations 1-3), and on disk the code this plan reuses: EXP8 lib/ego.py (concept_core, windows, density and persistence definitions), EXP8 passA.py (the snapshot pass and its 62-minute runtime), the EXP8 README runtime table, EXP7 method.py state_panel() and lib/h2_exp6.states(), and the EXP5 frame_concepts.csv schema. No new literature search was run in this planning session: its prompt arrived truncated, and the novelty check is the sibling Research artifact's job. The econometric practice below is the standard panel/event-study canon the strategy itself cites (Sun & Abraham 2021; Callaway & Sant'Anna 2021) plus well-established companions. The citations are to be verified with aii-semscholar-bib at write-up.\\n\\nWITHIN-UNIT TIMING DESIGNS. In science-of-science and diffusion work, a claim that X precedes Y within units is expected to use unit and time fixed effects, a count model suited to many zeros, and an explicit reverse-path or lead test. The usual count model is Poisson pseudo-maximum likelihood with high-dimensional FE (Santos Silva & Tenreyro 2006; Wooldridge 1999 on the consistency of FE Poisson under only a correct conditional mean; Correia, Guimaraes & Zylkin 2020 ppmlhdfe; pyfixest fepois in Python). Linear probability models on binary hazards are the robustness twin. Lagged outcomes in short FE panels create Nickell (1981) bias, so the cumulative-entries control is kept out of the primary model.\\n\\nSTAGGERED EVENT STUDIES. Since 2020, two-way-FE event studies with staggered timing and heterogeneous effects are known to be biased: already-treated units act as controls with negative weights (Goodman-Bacon 2021; de Chaisemartin & D'Haultfoeuille 2020; Sun & Abraham 2021; Callaway & Sant'Anna 2021; Borusyak, Jaravel & Spiess 2024). Reviewers now expect a heterogeneity-robust estimator, never-treated or not-yet-treated controls stated explicitly, and pre-trend tests. They also expect awareness that pre-trend tests have low power: Roth (2022) recommends reporting what violation the test could detect.\\n\\nCONTROLS SPECIFIC TO EGO-NETWORK MEASURES. Ego-network density falls mechanically with ego size, so density comparisons need a size control or a size-matched null. The configuration/degree-matched null is standard in network science (e.g. Maslov & Sneppen 2002; the structural-diversity literature Ugander et al. 2012 conditions on neighbourhood size). In co-occurrence emergence work, the 'density vs centrality' strategic diagram (Callon, Courtial & Laville 1991) is the classic lens. Because density there is a THEME property, a 'closure' measure must separate a genuine tightening of the neighbourhood from simply having fewer papers. Hence the home-volume controls, the log degree, the dens_adj null and the home-volume event-study check.\\n\\nMECHANICAL COUPLING. A diffusion outcome and a network indicator built from the same papers are coupled. The standard remedy is to build the predictor from a disjoint set: here, home-venue papers predict off-home venue entries. The ALL-PAPERS build is kept only as the demonstration of that coupling.\\n\\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Panel studies of topic or field dynamics typically have thousands of units and 10-20 periods. Here there are about 12.5k concepts x up to 11 years (about 100-120k usable rows), which is ample for FE Poisson. The binding constraint is the number of closure-jump events per cohort in the event study, which is checked before the seal (F6). Effects on record for static openness are small (partial rho about 0.08-0.17), so within-concept effects of a similar standardised size (about 3-10% change in the entry rate per within-concept SD) are the realistic target. CIs, not p-values alone, are reported, with per-group results and DL-pooled I2 as in the run's practice.\\n\\nMEASUREMENT DETAIL. Topic typing by LLM needs a benchmark: two-model agreement plus hand checks, reported with kappa, as is now expected for LLM annotation in computational social science. OpenAlex topics (2024 taxonomy, back-assigned) are a known anachronism for early years. The run's EXP3 backbone uses them throughout, so the anachronism is shared by features and background and is stated as a limitation.\",\n  \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(1) Concept and calendar-year FE with PPML for the count outcome, an LPM twin for the binary hazard, concept-clustered CIs and a 2,000-draw concept-cluster refit bootstrap.\\n(2) The reverse path is estimated with the same FE and compared on a within-concept standardised scale with a paired bootstrap, so 'openness leads spread' is tested against 'spread leads openness'.\\n(3) A heterogeneity-robust Sun-Abraham event study with never-treated and not-yet-treated controls, a pre-trend joint test plus a Roth-style power statement, and two permutation placebos. A plain TWFE event study is shown only in the simulation unit test to demonstrate its bias.\\n(4) Ego-density size confounding is handled by a log-degree control and a degree-matched null (dens_adj) robustness, and the home-volume shock check keeps a 'closure jump' from being a volume dip.\\n(5) Mechanical coupling is removed by construction: home-venue papers build the predictor, off-home D3 entries are the outcome. The ALL-PAPERS build is reported only as a contrast.\\n(6) Nickell bias is avoided by keeping the lagged cumulative outcome out of the primary model (it enters only in sensitivity S2). The age/year/concept collinearity is stated and handled by swapping the FE (S1).\\n(7) The pre-registration is sealed before outcomes are attached, with explicit verdict rules, Holm over the two primary tests, and per-body and per-group reporting with DL pooling and I2.\\n(8) LLM topic typing is benchmarked (two-model kappa and hand accuracy) with a pre-declared lexical fallback.\\n\\nDEPARTURES AND THEIR COST:\\n(a) The outcomes are not virgin. EXP7/EXP8 already examined D3 states and static breadth for these concepts, so the seal protects only the new within-concept estimand. Cost: this is mechanism evidence, not confirmation. It is labelled as such, as the strategy requires, and the sibling Art 1 carries the confirmatory burden.\\n(b) Yearly z constants for OPEN_home are frozen here on DEV concept-year features rather than taken from Art 1's static constants, because Art 1 runs in parallel and the yearly components are on a different scale. Cost: OPEN_home(t) is not numerically identical to Art 1's OPEN; the sign convention and components are the same.\\n(c) Years >= 2015 use the 2010-14 backbone slice (the EXP3 backbone ends in 2014). Cost: community labels for late years are slightly stale. There is a sensitivity dropping those rows.\\n(d) 1-year windows make yearly ego statistics noisy for small concepts. The F4 threshold rule, decided on features before the seal, trades noise against coverage.\\n(e) Causal language is limited. Concept FE absorb time-invariant confounders (type, footprint, home field), but time-varying shocks (e.g. a field-wide funding surge that raises home density and lowers off-home entry) are not ruled out. Year FE absorb only common shocks. A home-field x year FE sensitivity is feasible and is added to the robustness list if runtime allows (it is the first item after the F8 drop list). The paper will say 'precedes and predicts within concept', not 'causes'.\\n(f) Callaway & Sant'Anna is not run separately. Sun-Abraham with never-treated controls identifies the same cohort-time ATTs under the same parallel-trends assumption, and a second estimator would cost time with little extra information. A reviewer may still ask for it, and the code layout permits adding it in iteration 5.\\n(g) The partner-decomposition psp values are exploratory, on old held-out outcomes already unsealed. They explain the static signal; they do not re-confirm it.\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}

Context via ego_ctx with EXP8 bg_topics.npz.\\nworks_home(ci)  = [(year, topic_idx)] for grounded works with vfield in home_list (vfield == 0 unlabelled -> excluded;\\n                  log home-paper coverage = home works / all grounded works per concept-year)\\nworks_all(ci)   = all grounded works (for the ALL-PAPERS comparison build)\\nSELF(ci) = ego.self_topics(name, aliases, counts over t0..t0+2 of works_all)   # frozen once per concept, as in EXP8\\ns(t) = slice_of(t) using EXP3 SLICES; years >= 2015 clamp to slice 2 (2010-14) -> logged; sensitivity drops those rows\\nfor t in t0..h_end:\\n   NB(t)   = neighbours(cnt_home[t], nc_home[t], bg[t], GT[t], SELF, min_n = 2)    # 1-year window, as EXP8 W1..W3\\n   SEEN(t) = union of topics with >= 1 home count in t0-3..t-1\\n   NEW(t)  = NB(t) & ~SEEN(t)\\n   new_rate(t)   = |NEW(t)| / (|NB(t-1)| + 1)                                      # yearly analogue of new_edge_rate\\n   n_comm(t)     = #distinct comm[s(t)] labels among NB(t)\\n   participation(t) = 1 - sum_c w_c^2, with w_c = count-weighted share of NB(t) in community c\\n   nov_res(t)    = share of NEW(t) outside the concept's first-year (t0) modal community, minus the degree-weighted\\n                   expectation over the pool (EXP8 NOV_res formula, applied to NEW(t))\\n   density(t)    = (#full_edges[s(t)] among NB(t)) / C(|NB(t)|, 2)      (NA if |NB(t)| < 2)\\n   dens_null(t)  = mean density of 100 random topic sets of size |NB(t)| drawn with bg[t]-proportional weights from the\\n                   non-SELF pool (Gumbel top-k as in ego.distinct_null); dens_adj(t) = density(t) - dens_null(t)\\n   persistence(t)= Jaccard(NB(t-1), NB(t))\\n   deg(t) = |NB(t)|, kcore(t) = coreness of the concept node inserted into the kNN graph of s(t) (igraph coreness only)\\n   controls: log1p home works(t), log1p all works(t), log1p deg(t), log(at_risk(t)), concept age a = t - t0\\n   ALSO for works_all: density_all(t), new_rate_all(t) (the ALL-PAPERS build, for comparison only)\\nOPEN_home(t) = mean of z(new_rate), z(n_comm), z(participation), z(nov_res), -z(density), -z(persistence); needs >= 4 of 6.\\n   The z constants are the mean and SD over DEV concept-years, computed from FEATURE columns only and frozen in step 4.\\n   (DEVIATION: the strategy asked for Art 1's EXP5 static constants. Art 1 runs in parallel and the yearly components\\n   have a different scale, so own constants are frozen here and logged.)\\nParallelise over concepts: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers), context loaded once per worker; chunks of 200 concepts;\\n   checkpoint per chunk. Timing test on 100 concepts first (T4) -> extrapolate.\\nWRITE WS/data/yearly_features.parquet (no outcome columns).\\nPORT CHECK: aggregating ego_year over t0..t0+2 with EXP8 definitions (windows W1..W3 = t0, t0+1, t0+2, all papers)\\n   must reproduce EXP8 ego_features new_edge_rate, n_comm_W3, participation, ego_density_W3, edge_persistence to 1e-9 on\\n   300 random concepts. Otherwise STOP and fix the port.\\n\\n# ======================= 4. PRE-REGISTRATION AND SEAL (before any outcome join) =======================\\nWS/prereg.md + WS/results/frozen_spec.json:\\n   panel definition, the yearly formulas, the yearly z constants, the closure-jump rule, the take-off rule, the prominence\\n   definition, the estimators, controls, FE, clustering, bootstrap B and seed (20260929), Holm families, the predictions\\n   and verdict rules below, the sha256 of every WS/lib/*.py file and of yearly_features.parquet.\\n   sha256(frozen_spec.json) -> WS/logs/seal.log; git commit. ONLY THEN does the estimation script attach D3 outcomes\\n   (lib/seal.py gate: attach_outcomes() raises unless seal.log matches frozen_spec).\\n   HONEST NOTE in prereg.md: EXP7/EXP8 already looked at D3 states and static breadth for these concepts. The within-\\n   concept yearly estimand was never estimated, and this seal controls only that.\\nPREDICTIONS (frozen):\\n   H-M1 (primary, DEV): PPML beta_density < 0 (entries(t+1) on density(t)), concept-clustered 95% CI < 0.\\n   H-M2 (primary, DEV): PPML beta_OPEN > 0 (entries(t+1) on OPEN_home(t)), 95% CI > 0.\\n        Holm over {H-M1, H-M2}.\\n   H-M3 (reverse path weaker): |std beta_fwd| - |std beta_rev| > 0 with a paired concept-bootstrap 95% CI > 0, where\\n        the reverse is density(t+1) on entries(t) and the standardisation uses within-concept SDs.\\n   H-M4 (event study): after the first closure jump, entries fall (mean of lags 0..+2 < 0, CI < 0); pre-trend joint Wald\\n        test on leads -3..-1 has p > 0.10 AND max |lead| < 0.5 x |mean lag 0..+2|; the event-date permutation p < 0.05.\\n   H-M5 (replication bodies): the signs of H-M1 and H-M2 hold on OLD_HELDOUT and COHORT (point estimates, CI reported).\\n   VERDICT: MECHANISM SUPPORTED = H-M1 & H-M2 (Holm) & H-M3 & H-M4 & H-M5 signs;\\n            PARTIAL = H-M1 or H-M2 holds but H-M3 or H-M4 fails (report which);\\n            NOT SUPPORTED = both H-M1 and H-M2 CIs include 0 on DEV.\\n   H-S1 (sequence): intersection-born concepts take off WITHOUT a prior home-prominence peak more often than single-home\\n        concepts (share difference > 0, concept-bootstrap CI > 0).\\n   H-P1 (partners, exploratory): method-topic and new-community partners carry more of the new_edge_rate signal (larger\\n        psp with O2r_m50 given B5) than domain-topic and same-community partners.\\n\\n# ======================= 5a. TOPIC TYPING (LLM; parallel to Pass M; cap $1) =======================\\ntopics = the distinct topic ids that appear as NEW partners in any concept's t0..t0+2 window, from EXP8\\n   frame_matches_early (expected about 4.5k of 4,516 -> label all 4,516; still cheap)\\nprompt (JSON output): topic display name + subfield + field + its 5 top keywords (from topic_meta if present, else name\\n   only) -> {'class': 'METHOD' (technique, instrument, algorithm, analytic approach) | 'DOMAIN' (phenomenon, object,\\n   organism, material, disease, place, population), 'confidence': 0-1}\\nmodel A = a cheap OpenRouter model selected via aii-openrouter-llms by price (e.g. a Gemini Flash-Lite / GPT mini class\\n   model); temperature 0; batches of 40 topics per call; asyncio + Semaphore(8); running cost from usage.cost; stop at $0.80\\n   or on the first 'AI Inventor per-run OpenRouter budget' 403.\\nBENCHMARK: 100 stratified topics double-labelled by model B (a different family); 40 hand-checked by the executor\\n   (write the hand labels in results/topic_type_handcheck.csv BEFORE viewing the model labels).\\n   Required: Cohen kappa(A, B) >= 0.6 AND accuracy vs hand >= 0.85. If this fails: revise the prompt once; if it fails\\n   again, keep only topics where A == B and log it.\\n-> WS/results/topic_types.csv (topic_id, name, field, class, confidence, model, agree_B)\\n\\n# ======================= 5b. PRIMARY FE ESTIMATION (about 45 min incl. bootstraps) =======================\\npanel = yearly_features JOIN D3 (ci, t) -> outcome y(t+1) = entries(t+1), any_entry(t+1)\\nsample rows: t0 <= t <= h_end - 1; at_risk(t) > 0; home-only deg(t) >= 2 (density defined); report the dropped share\\nEstimator (pyfixest.fepois): y(t+1) ~ X(t) + log1p_home_works(t) + log1p_all_works(t) + log1p_deg(t) + log_at_risk(t)\\n                                  | concept FE + calendar-year FE ; vcov = CRV1 by concept\\n   X in {density, OPEN_home} as SEPARATE models (plus a joint model reported as secondary)\\n   Concepts with all-zero y are dropped by PPML (report n).\\n   NOTE: age = year - t0 is collinear with concept FE + year FE, so age is absorbed. Sensitivity S1 replaces year FE with\\n   age FE (the linear trend is then absorbed instead).\\n   NOTE: cum_entries(t-1) is NOT in the primary model (it is a lagged outcome; Nickell-type bias with T <= 11).\\n   Sensitivity S2 adds it.\\nBinary hazard: LPM with the same FE (pyfixest.feols) on any_entry(t+1); reported alongside.\\nBootstrap: 2,000 concept-cluster resamples (resample concepts with replacement, relabel duplicates as new FE units,\\n   refit) -> percentile 95% CI; joblib 6 workers. (With ~100k rows one fepois refit takes about 1-2 s -> about 10 min.)\\nReverse path: density(t+1) ~ entries(t) + same controls at t+1 | concept FE + year FE (feols); standardise both betas by\\n   within-concept SDs (the SD of the FE-demeaned variables); paired bootstrap of |std fwd| - |std rev| (same resampled\\n   concepts in both).\\nBodies: DEV (primary), OLD_HELDOUT, COHORT, each separately; per group within each body -> DL pooling with I2 over groups.\\nRobustness (all pre-declared): dens_adj instead of density; exclude Medicine homes; exclude intersection-born; drop rows with\\n   year >= 2015 (backbone clamp); ALL-PAPERS density instead of home-only (contrast: shows how much the all-papers version\\n   is mechanically coupled); exclude rows where home coverage < 0.5; Poisson with log exposure = log at_risk as an offset\\n   instead of a control.\\n\\n# ======================= 5c. EVENT STUDY (about 30 min) =======================\\nwithin-concept SD of density over t0..h_end (needs >= 5 defined years)\\nclosure jump at t* = the FIRST t with age >= 2 and density(t) - density(t-1) >= 1 within-concept SD (and deg(t) >= 3)\\ncohort g = t*; never-treated = concepts with no jump; relative time e = t - g in -3..+4 (bins: <= -4 and >= +5 are\\n   binned and not reported)\\nSun & Abraham (2021) interaction-weighted estimator, implemented directly:\\n   regress y(t+1) (and separately entries(t)) on sum_g sum_{e != -1} 1[G = g] x 1[t - g = e] + controls\\n   | concept FE + year FE, with never-treated concepts as the control; then average the cohort-specific CATT(g, e) with\\n   weights = the cohort shares among treated at each e. SEs by concept-cluster bootstrap (1,000).\\n   Alternative control = the last-treated cohort (not-yet-treated); report both.\\n   Cross-check: pyfixest event_study(estimator='saturated') if it exists in the installed version, else a statsmodels\\n   OLS with the same dummies; the point estimates must agree to 1e-6.\\nPre-trends: joint Wald test of leads -3..-2 (e = -1 omitted); also report Roth (2022)-style: the largest linear pre-trend\\n   the test has 80% power to detect, given the lead SEs.\\nPlacebos: (i) the event year is permuted within concept among eligible years (1,000 draws) -> the null distribution of the\\n   mean lag 0..+2 effect; (ii) within-concept-year permutation of which off-home field was entered (keeps the counts,\\n   breaks field identity; used on the partner-community check that entries move to fields outside the new partners'\\n   communities).\\nPlaceholders for mechanical checks: the same event study with home works(t) as the outcome (a closure jump should NOT\\n   be simply a home-volume shock); report it.\\n\\n# ======================= 6. WHY IT WORKS: PARTNER-SOURCE DECOMPOSITION (early window t0..t0+2; about 30 min) ============\\nfor each concept, the static new partners NEW = the EXP8 new-neighbour set over W1..W3 (all papers, the EXP8 definition,\\n   recomputed by ego.concept_core with windows = rq1_windows; betweenness off) with, per new partner k:\\n   type_k      = METHOD | DOMAIN (topic_types.csv)\\n   pfield_k    = home | off-home (topic_meta field of k, mapped to the 26 venue-field codes; home_list)\\n   comm_new_k  = comm[s(first_year_k)][k] != the concept's first-year modal community\\n   carrier_k   = home | off-home | mixed (the venue fields of the concept papers in which k first appears)\\nclass-restricted indicators: new_edge_rate_X = (|NEW & X| / 3) / (n1 + 1) and n_comm_W3_X = #communities among W3\\n   neighbours in class X, for X in {METHOD, DOMAIN, pfield home, pfield off-home, comm_new, comm_old, carrier home,\\n   carrier off-home}\\nscore (EXPLORATORY; old held-out outcomes already unsealed): partial Spearman of each with O2r_m50 and O2r_resid given\\n   B5 + group + t0 dummies (EXP8 rq1stats psp, identical code), 2,000 concept bootstraps; per body (DEV, OLD_HELDOUT,\\n   COHORT) and DL-pooled over the 4 held-out groups with I2.\\n   Paired bootstrap differences: METHOD - DOMAIN, comm_new - comm_old, carrier home - carrier off-home.\\n   KEY QUESTION: does new_edge_rate restricted to partners delivered by HOME-venue papers keep psp > 0? If yes, the\\n   signal is not a by-product of the concept already being in off-home venues.\\nBRIDGING PAPERS: concept papers in t0..t0+2 that introduce >= 1 new partner from a community new to the concept.\\n   Per concept: bridging share, and for bridging vs other early papers: median team size, share of authors new to the\\n   concept (no earlier concept paper), review share (doc_type), home vs off-home venue share.\\n   Test: psp(bridging_share, O2r_m50 | B5) per body.\\n-> WS/results/partner_decomposition.json, WS/data/bridging_papers.parquet\\n\\n# ======================= 7. SEQUENCE TEST (RQ2; about 20 min) =======================\\nprominence(ci, t) = the mean of the within-home percentile ranks of the home-only deg(t) and kcore(t), among all frame\\n   concepts sharing the same primary home field in calendar year t (ranks with >= 20 concepts per home-year, else NA)\\nprominence peak = argmax_t prominence over t0..h_end (ties -> earliest); peak must exceed the concept's median by >= 20\\n   percentile points to count as a peak (else 'no peak')\\ntake-off T = the first t with entries(t) >= 2 (off-home, D3); concepts without a take-off are censored\\nTests:\\n   (a) share with peak < T among concepts with a take-off: single-home vs intersection-born; concept bootstrap CI of the\\n       difference (H-S1)\\n   (b) time to take-off: Kaplan-Meier by single vs multi home; Cox model with group strata and log early volume\\n   (c) two event studies with the Sun-Abraham code of 5c: outcome entries around the peak year; outcome prominence around T.\\n       Pre-trends for both.\\n   Report by body, and excluding Medicine homes.\\n-> WS/results/sequence_tests.json\\n\\n# ======================= 8. FIGURES, AUDIT, OUTPUTS (about 30 min) =======================\\nfigures (PNG + PDF, via aii-data-fig-gen house style):\\n   fig_fe_coefs: forest plot of the standardised forward vs reverse betas, per body and group\\n   fig_event_closure: event-study coefficients -3..+4 with 95% CIs (never-treated and not-yet-treated controls), plus the\\n     placebo band\\n   fig_partner_decomp: psp by partner class with CIs (DEV, old held-out)\\n   fig_sequence: KM curves by single vs multi home + the prominence -> take-off event study\\n   fig_panel_example: 4 matched concepts (Art 3 overlap allowed): yearly density, OPEN_home and entries\\naudit.py (an independent code path): recompute H-M1 and H-M2 with statsmodels GLM Poisson + explicit concept and year\\n   dummies on a 1,000-concept DEV subsample -> the same betas as pyfixest to 1e-5; recompute 3 Sun-Abraham cells by\\n   hand; planted-effect and shuffled controls (T7).\\nWrite: yearly_panel.parquet (features + outcomes, after the seal), fe_results.json, event_study.json, partner_decomposition.json,\\n   topic_types.csv, topic_type_benchmark.json, bridging_papers.parquet, sequence_tests.json, frozen_spec.json + logs/seal.log,\\n   deviations.json, provenance.json, pipeline_counts.json (works, concept-years, rows at each filter, events, per body) for\\n   the paper's methodology figure, and method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out): one example per concept-year row of the\\n   estimation sample; input = the X(t) vector and controls; output = entries(t+1); predict_fe_density / predict_fe_open /\\n   predict_controls_only = fitted values from the three models (DEV fitted in 5 concept folds, out-of-fold);\\n   metadata = ci, body, group, t, age. Full/mini/preview via aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if > limit.\\nREADME.md: layout, how to run, the verdict table (H-M1..H-M5, H-S1, H-P1 with the deciding quantities), the deviations,\\n   and 'Restoring removed files'.\\n.aii/manifest.yaml: keep data/yearly_panel.parquet, data/yearly_features.parquet, data/frame_matches_long/ (regenerable but\\n   costly), results/, figures/; delete .venv/, passM/parts/, __pycache__/.\",", "+  \"implementation_pseudocode\": \"# ======================= 0. SETUP (about 15 min) =======================\\n# Read the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-use-hardware, aii-json, aii-file-size-limit\\n# and aii-openrouter-llms first. WS = this workspace; R = run root. Every write goes under WS. The OpenAlex API is NOT used\\n# (0 credits); only the public S3 snapshot via HTTP range reads.\\nuv venv; uv pip install numpy pandas pyarrow scipy scikit-learn statsmodels pyfixest python-igraph leidenalg loguru\\n   matplotlib pyahocorasick nltk joblib\\n# Copy (do not import across workspaces) into WS/lib/, recording sha256 per copy in WS/results/provenance.json:\\n#   EXP8: passA.py -> passM.py, lib/{matcher,rangefile,common,common5,common3,ego,ego_ctx,stats_core,seal}.py\\n#   EXP7: lib/d3.py, lib/h2_exp6.py\\n#   EXP3: backbone/slice*.npz, scan/topic_ids.json, results/topic_meta.csv\\n#   EXP5: lexicon_v1.parquet (check the sha against frozen_lexicon.sha256), results/source_field.parquet,\\n#         snapshot/works_manifest.json\\nframe = read_csv(EXP5/frame_concepts.csv)   # assert 12,499 rows; split x group counts == {DEV 4771, PHYS 742,\\n                                             # LIFEENV 1113, SOC 1352, MATHDEC 165, COHORT 4356}\\nframe['home_list'] = [int(h) for h in home.split('|')]; frame['multi_home'] = intersect40 == 1\\nframe['body'] = DEV | OLD_HELDOUT (split == HELDOUT) | COHORT\\nframe['h_end'] = min(t0 + 10, 2022)        # COHORT concepts have shorter panels (t0 2010-14 -> 8-12 years); logged\\n\\n# ======================= 1. PASS M: one zero-credit snapshot pass (about 70-90 min, in the BACKGROUND) =======================\\n# Adapt EXP8 passA.process_file. The base filter, automaton, stemmed verification, TAG_MIN = 0.3 and venue lookup are\\n# UNCHANGED. Changes:\\n#   (i) title matching for publication years 2000..2022 (was 2000..2016)\\n#   (ii) keep rows for frame hits with tagstate == 1 and t0 - 3 <= year <= min(t0 + 10, 2022)\\n#   (iii) add the column 'type' (article | review) to the emitted row\\n#   (iv) authors for ALL kept years (was year >= t0 only); cited_by_count is dropped (not needed)\\n#   (v) BG/GT are NOT recomputed; reuse EXP8 data/bg_topics.npz (1995-2022, same filter) -> faster\\nCOLS_M = passA.COLS minus 'cited_by_count' plus 'type'\\nrow = (ci int32, year int16, work_id int64, vfield int8, doc_type int8 {0 article, 1 review}, topic_idx list[int16],\\n       author_ids list[int64])\\nwrite WS/passM/parts/part_{fi}.parquet + done_{fi}.json (resumable); run with ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers),\\n   8 range-read threads per worker; PID file + nohup; poll the log every 5 min\\nmerge -> WS/data/frame_matches_long/part_*.parquet (each < 90 MB). Expected: 12.5k concepts x about 600 works = about\\n   7-8M rows, about 0.6-1.0 GB of parquet -> kept as parts; aii-file-size-limit applies.\\nCHECK M1 (STOP rule): per concept, grounded counts per (year, vfield) from Pass M must EQUAL EXP5 agg_counts (tagstate == 1)\\n   for every year <= 2022, for >= 99.5% of concept-years; the median per-concept Spearman over years must be >= 0.999.\\n   Otherwise STOP, diagnose (lexicon sha, automaton, year window, base filter) and rerun on 5 files before continuing.\\nCHECK M2: for t0-3..t0+2 the (ci, work_id) set equals EXP8 frame_matches_early for >= 99.5% of rows, and the topic lists\\n   are identical.\\n# While Pass M runs: write steps 2-4 and the unit tests; run the topic typing (step 5a), which needs no pass.\\n\\n# ======================= 2. D3 STATE PANEL (about 10 min) =======================\\nsp = concat(EXP7 state_panel_dev.parquet, state_panel_heldout.parquet)\\nmissing = frame concepts absent from sp (EXP7's DEV panel may be 'EXP5 minus EXP6'; log the count)\\nfor missing concepts: G = agg_counts (tagstate == 1) -> [NY, 27] per concept; states via d3.panel_states / h2_exp6.states\\n   (min_n = 2), the SAME code; VALIDATE by recomputing 200 random concepts already in sp: every cell must be equal.\\nPer concept-year t, over off-home fields:\\n   entries(t)   = #fields whose state switches to 'entered' (cum reaches 2) in year t\\n   any_entry(t) = entries(t) > 0\\n   at_risk(t)   = #off-home fields still untouched at the end of t-1  (the risk-set size; 26 - |home| - already entered)\\n   cum_entries(t-1), retained(t), lost(t)\\n\\n# ======================= 3. YEARLY HOME-ONLY EGO PANEL (features only; about 40 min on 6 workers) =======================\\n# A new function ego_year(ci, t) in WS/lib/ego_yearly.py, built from ego.py primitives (neighbours, pmi, bg_window,\\n# window_counts, self_topics). NO betweenness. Context via ego_ctx with EXP8 bg_topics.npz.\\nworks_home(ci)  = [(year, topic_idx)] for grounded works with vfield in home_list (vfield == 0 unlabelled -> excluded;\\n                  log home-paper coverage = home works / all grounded works per concept-year)\\nworks_all(ci)   = all grounded works (for the ALL-PAPERS comparison build)\\nSELF(ci) = ego.self_topics(name, aliases, counts over t0..t0+2 of works_all)   # frozen once per concept, as in EXP8\\ns(t) = slice_of(t) using EXP3 SLICES; years >= 2015 clamp to slice 2 (2010-14) -> logged; sensitivity drops those rows\\nfor t in t0..h_end:\\n   NB(t)   = neighbours(cnt_home[t], nc_home[t], bg[t], GT[t], SELF, min_n = 2)    # 1-year window, as EXP8 W1..W3\\n   SEEN(t) = union of topics with >= 1 home count in t0-3..t-1\\n   NEW(t)  = NB(t) & ~SEEN(t)\\n   new_rate(t)   = |NEW(t)| / (|NB(t-1)| + 1)                                      # yearly analogue of new_edge_rate\\n   n_comm(t)     = #distinct comm[s(t)] labels among NB(t)\\n   participation(t) = 1 - sum_c w_c^2, with w_c = count-weighted share of NB(t) in community c\\n   nov_res(t)    = share of NEW(t) outside the concept's first-year (t0) modal community, minus the degree-weighted\\n                   expectation over the pool (EXP8 NOV_res formula, applied to NEW(t))\\n   density(t)    = (#full_edges[s(t)] among NB(t)) / C(|NB(t)|, 2)      (NA if |NB(t)| < 2)\\n   dens_null(t)  = mean density of 100 random topic sets of size |NB(t)| drawn with bg[t]-proportional weights from the\\n                   non-SELF pool (Gumbel top-k as in ego.distinct_null); dens_adj(t) = density(t) - dens_null(t)\\n   persistence(t)= Jaccard(NB(t-1), NB(t))\\n   deg(t) = |NB(t)|, kcore(t) = coreness of the concept node inserted into the kNN graph of s(t) (igraph coreness only)\\n   controls: log1p home works(t), log1p all works(t), log1p deg(t), log(at_risk(t)), concept age a = t - t0\\n   ALSO for works_all: density_all(t), new_rate_all(t) (the ALL-PAPERS build, for comparison only)\\nOPEN_home(t) = mean of z(new_rate), z(n_comm), z(participation), z(nov_res), -z(density), -z(persistence); needs >= 4 of 6.\\n   The z constants are the mean and SD over DEV concept-years, computed from FEATURE columns only and frozen in step 4.\\n   (DEVIATION: the strategy asked for Art 1's EXP5 static constants. Art 1 runs in parallel and the yearly components\\n   have a different scale, so own constants are frozen here and logged.)\\nParallelise over concepts: ProcessPoolExecutor(spawn, 6 workers), context loaded once per worker; chunks of 200 concepts;\\n   checkpoint per chunk. Timing test on 100 concepts first (T4) -> extrapolate.\\nWRITE WS/data/yearly_features.parquet (no outcome columns).\\nPORT CHECK: aggregating ego_year over t0..t0+2 with EXP8 definitions (windows W1..W3 = t0, t0+1, t0+2, all papers)\\n   must reproduce EXP8 ego_features new_edge_rate, n_comm_W3, participation, ego_density_W3, edge_persistence to 1e-9 on\\n   300 random concepts. Otherwise STOP and fix the port.\\n\\n# ======================= 4. ONLY THEN does the estimation script attach D3 outcomes\\n   (lib/seal.py gate: attach_outcomes() raises unless seal.log matches frozen_spec).\\n   HONEST NOTE in prereg.md: EXP7/EXP8 already looked at D3 states and static breadth for these concepts. In science-of-science and diffusion work, a claim that X precedes Y within units is expected to use unit and time fixed effects, a count model suited to many zeros, and an explicit reverse-path or lead test. The usual count model is Poisson pseudo-maximum likelihood with high-dimensional FE (Santos Silva & Tenreyro 2006; Wooldridge 1999 on the consistency of FE Poisson under only a correct conditional mean; Correia, Guimaraes & Zylkin 2020 ppmlhdfe; pyfixest fepois in Python). Since 2020, two-way-FE event studies with staggered timing and heterogeneous effects are known to be biased: already-treated units act as controls with negative weights (Goodman-Bacon 2021; de Chaisemartin & D'Haultfoeuille 2020; Sun & Abraham 2021; Callaway & Sant'Anna 2021; Borusyak, Jaravel & Spiess 2024). They also expect awareness that pre-trend tests have low power: Roth (2022) recommends reporting what violation the test could detect.\\n\\nCONTROLS SPECIFIC TO EGO-NETWORK MEASURES. Ego-network density falls mechanically with ego size, so density comparisons need a size control or a size-matched null. The configuration/degree-matched null is standard in network science (e.g. Maslov & Sneppen 2002; the structural-diversity literature Ugander et al. 2012 conditions on neighbourhood size). In co-occurrence emergence work, the 'density vs centrality' strategic diagram (Callon, Courtial & Laville 1991) is the classic lens. Because density there is a THEME property, a 'closure' measure must separate a genuine tightening of the neighbourhood from simply having fewer papers. The standard remedy is to build the predictor from a disjoint set: here, home-venue papers predict off-home venue entries. The ALL-PAPERS build is kept only as the demonstration of that coupling.\\n\\nHOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Panel studies of topic or field dynamics typically have thousands of units and 10-20 periods. The binding constraint is the number of closure-jump events per cohort in the event study, which is checked before the seal (F6). CIs, not p-values alone, are reported, with per-group results and DL-pooled I2 as in the run's practice.\\n\\nMEASUREMENT DETAIL. Topic typing by LLM needs a benchmark: two-model agreement plus hand checks, reported with kappa, as is now expected for LLM annotation in computational social science. Your prompt arrived cut off: it began mid-sentence ("…d work if available, produced results…") and was missing the planning instructions and the output format. I went ahead on an assumption. This directory, `gen_plan_experiment_2`, matches the second experiment in this iteration's strategy, the within-concept mechanism test of the "open early neighbourhoods" lead. I wrote a plan for it in the same format as earlier plans in this run:

`.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json` (in `iter_4/gen_plan/gen_plan_experiment_2/`)

I couldn't run a JSON parser here because there was no shell. I checked the escaping by hand, but a quick `python -m json.tool` on the file before the pipeline reads it would be worth doing.

**What the plan does:**
- **Data:** it makes one free pass over the OpenAlex bulk snapshot, reusing the earlier matching code unchanged but extending the window to ten years after onset. Pass counts must exactly reproduce the counts from earlier experiments before any features are built. That gives a table of about 12.5k concepts × up to 11 years.
- **Measurement:** each concept's yearly co-occurrence network is built only from papers in its home field, while the outcome counts new outside fields entered. Because the two use different papers, the measure can't simply reflect the spread it's predicting.
- **Main tests:**
  - A count model with concept and year fixed effects tests whether a concept's home neighbourhood closing up in year t is followed by fewer new-field entries in t+1.
  - The reverse direction (entries leading to later closure) must be weaker.
  - An event study around the first sharp closure checks for flat trends before the event, plus shuffled-date and shuffled-field placebos.
  - The predictions and verdict rules are locked and hashed before the outcome columns are joined.
- **Why it works:**
  - The early "new partners" signal is split by source: method vs domain topics (labelled by an LLM, checked against a second model and by hand, at most $1), old vs new topic communities, and home vs outside papers.
  - A profile of "bridging" papers, the ones that bring in the new partners.
- **Sequence question from your brief:** does a concept become prominent in its home field before spreading, and do concepts born across two fields spread without that peak?
